# Open issues

*The single list of known open items for the project (project standard 10). First compiled 2026-11-18 by a sweep of every file in `notes/`, `notes/conjectures/`, `src/`, and the project-folder survey `catt-survey.md`.*

## How to use this file

- **Every thread that finds an open item adds a row here, and every thread that closes one moves it to "Closed" with a pointer to the result.** Do this in the same PR as the finding.
- **Status** is one of:
  - **in progress**: an active thread has this item on its checklist;
  - **out to researcher**: sent to an external researcher (internal attempts still continue, per standard 2);
  - **unowned**: nobody is working on it. Under standard 0 the coordinator starts a thread for it.
  - **blocked**: cannot advance until another item closes. The blocker is named, and adjacent work is listed.
- **Owner** is a thread, named by title and id. A thread that has finished its task does not own anything: items it left behind are listed as unowned, with the finished thread named as "former area" for context.
- IDs are stable. Do not renumber; retire an ID by moving its row to "Closed".

ν(K) is generated by atoms in this sense, `Strict/NuWords`); hypotheses checkable per basis element (`Strict/BasisCheck`, D¹ and D² instances); (N3): ν(λΓ) ≅ Free(Γ) done for ordered globular bases given with s, t and `Hyp` as parameters (`Strict/FreeGlob`: atom boundaries are atoms, `Glob.Free.functor` and `Glob.unique`, D² instance; λΓ is not built from Γ, and the order excludes e.g. α : f ⇒ g with β : g ⇒ f); the Gray cylinder K ⊗ λD¹ of an ordered unital basis is ordered unital (`Strict/Cylinder`: `CylDD.Hyp′`, atoms ⟨k⊗e⟩, ⟨k⊗i⟩ computed); λΓ with `Hyp` is built from any ordered globular presentation (`Strict/GlobBasis`: `GData`, `GB.hyp`, `FreeOn`, suspension `Σg`); and from every Batanin tree (`treeG`, wedge of suspensions); ν of a morphism of complexes is a strict functor (`Strict/NuMap`: `Hom` respects ∂ = ∂⁺ − ∂⁻, not ∂^± separately; `LinHom` from per-generator checks); the collapse q : λD ⊗ λD¹ → Σ λD is a `LinHom` with ν(q)⟨k⊗i⟩ = ⟨Σk⟩ (`Strict/Collapse`), and ν(Σ λD) is the quotient of ν(λD ⊗ λD¹) by its two ends: functors sending every ⟨k⊗0⟩ to one point and every ⟨k⊗1⟩ to another factor uniquely through ν(q) (`Strict/CollapseFree`, via `Strict/Kill`, `Strict/Split`, `Strict/CylSplit`, `Strict/GlobAtoms`); ν(λΓ) ≅ Free(Γ) for every pasting context Γ (`Strict/PsGlob`: the generators of `treeG (tr Γ)` are labelled bijectively by the variables of Γ, globularly; good labellings of Γ in 𝒟 and strict functors ν(λ(tr Γ)) → 𝒟 correspond, `FromGood.functor` with `FO.unique`, and for L ≥ the dimensions of the variables `Restrict` with both round trips); the tensor basis K ⊗ L of two ordered unital bases (with `DegP`, `DegM`) is ordered unital (`Strict/TensorBasis`: ∂∂ = 0 with the Koszul sign, degrees, augmentation, the layout with odd rows reversed and its order, `TensorAll.hyp⊗`; `Strict/TensorUnit`: Steiner's atom formula ⟨k⊗l⟩_d = Σ_a ⟨k⟩_a ⊗ ⟨l⟩_{d−a} with Koszul signs, unitality `unital⊗`, `Hyp⊗ : Hyp B⊗`), so ν(K)⊗ν(L) := ν(K⊗L) gets (N1), (N2); the strict model of L lands in `ωCat` cell by cell (`Strict/SortBag`: sorted bags, `BagADC.adc`; `Strict/TowerNu`: `cell→`, tower cells of dimension ≤ L are cells of `nu K L`; `Strict/StrictNu`: `isBasis` accepts exactly the basis (`basis→`, `basis←`), `term-cell`, every derivable t : arr A s u over a pasting context is a cell of ν(K(Γ)(x, y)) truncated at any L ≥ dim A); so every hom of the strict model is an `ωCat` containing the L-cells, and `OmegaSound.sound-in` applies to it (N3 link done); single-hom refinement done (`Strict/HomAgree`, with `Strict/HomTops`, `Strict/HomCore`, `Gray/CohDim.cohB<`): over a single hom Γ = ΣΘ, every derivable t : arr A s u of L (any derivation; its coh contexts may have many homs) lies in a hom x → x or 0 → 1 (`ends`), and its strict-model top chain topS t equals the top chain of its CaTT interpretation ⟦t⟧(η Γ) (`Strict/Interp`: σ-image of the sum of the top variables of Δ when dim A + 1 = dim Δ, else 0) in the hom 0 → 1 (`agree01`), while in a hom x → x the CaTT top is 0 (`agree`); so on one hom every coh image of the strict model, operation or coherence case, has the CaTT composite's top chain (top chains only; the lower levels of a tower are the top chains of its boundary terms, but no tower- or `tabOf`-level corollary is stated); open, META: the same for a hom x → y of a general pasting context Γ (not stated; `HomCore.Core` is already general in Δ, the induction uses ΣΘ through `SigmaFacts.ends01`, `short`) | META |

(The compactness, depth and n + m items are superseded by the r2 answer below.) Follow-up prompt `conjectures/answered/fibration-presentations-characterization-r2-2026-11-19.md` (answered 2026-11-19), and the homotopicality questions (bubble-free, one-sided, positive in every dimension, Chanavat Comment 3.40, Guetta Conj. 6.6.5) in `conjectures/answered/homotopy-polygraphs-2026-11-19.md` (both answered 2026-11-19). **Proved for set-valued weights over graphs** (discrete opfibration test, complete). **Proved for Cat-valued weights with finitely generated free values over a graph** (researcher, verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/finite-fibration-test-2026-11-18.md`): (Loc) holds in every positive dimension; FT₂ with vertical generators unmarked implies (★) and is complete for that class; the checker may index by the unlocalised computad. **FT₃ proved for positive 2-computad bases** (researcher, verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/finite-fibration-test-2d-base-2026-11-18.md`): Free(Γ) ≃ F₂(Γ) for positive Γ (Chanavat Cor. 3.39); λ's with prescribed target plus Ξ 3-generators present ∫T. Some non-positive bases (e.g. σ : 1_x ⇒ 1_x) admit **no** finite presentation of this form (L(B²ℕ) = K(ℤ,2) is not finite CW); round 2 below shows the right hypothesis is homotopicality, not positivity. FT over the walking 3-cell (researcher, verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/finite-fibration-test-higher-base-2026-11-18.md`): Cat-valued weights are presented by the FT₃ data plus ω_e : λ_{α,e} ⇛ λ_{β,e} per fibre vertex and a 4-generator K_{Ω,f} per fibre arrow (the generators are the relative Gray-tensor generators k ⊗ a of a universal total; ω_e alone fail by an Euler-characteristic count); 2-category-valued weights over the walking 2-cell need a 4-generator (necessity only); regularity is not necessary (explicit positive non-regular 3-computad with Free ≃ F₃). Open: sufficiency for 2-category-valued weights, general FT_n, which positive 3-computads are homotopical (positivity is settled as not necessary, below). Round 2 (researcher, verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/finite-fibration-test-higher-base-r2-2026-11-18.md`): **2-category-valued weights over the walking 2-cell are presented** by a double Gray cylinder (η, λ, κ, Ξ and one 4-generator Ω_ρ per fibre 2-cell, localised at η, κ, Ξ, Ω), via a tensor-localisation lemma X[S⁻¹] ⊗ I ≃ (X ⊗ I)[s⊗0, s⊗1, s⊗i]⁻¹; **positivity is not necessary**: (∗; t; η : 1 ⇒ t) is non-positive with Free ≃ F₂ = BΔ_+^inj, and its suspension is a non-positive homotopical 3-computad (so the σ : 1_x ⇒ 1_x obstruction is specific to that base; the rule's hypothesis should be "homotopical"). Open: general FT_n (gluing gives attaching maps only into the localised category; lifting them is the missing step), a criterion for homotopical 2-/3-computads, whether every positive 3-computad is homotopical. Round 3 (researcher, verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/finite-fibration-test-higher-base-r3-2026-11-18.md`): **attaching maps lift** by substituting along δ_r : I → [r], so **FT holds over every homotopical base of dimension ≤ 2 with fibres of any finite dimension m** (dimension ≤ m + 2); a one-vertex operadic criterion for homotopical 2-computads; the obstruction R = (∗; t; η : 1 ⇒ t, ε : t ⇒ 1) is not homotopical; a 3-dimensional attachment rule. Round 4 (verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/finite-fibration-test-higher-base-r4-2026-11-18.md`): FT₃ for a base 3-generator with composite boundary (Ω : α ⇛ β · γ; ω_x 3-generators, inverted K_f 4-generators); FT over every suspension base ΣQ with Q homotopical (dimension ≤ d + 2); a bubble (nonidentity 1_x ⇒ 1_x) rules out homotopicity, and bubble-freeness is undecidable; positivity must hold in every dimension; item-14 attachments extend to an incoming arrow. Open: FT₃ over non-suspension bases, FT_n with higher fibres, bubble-free ⇒ homotopical, positive 3-computads, general hom attachments. **FTX (2026-11-18, internal, not yet independently checked; `conjectures/fibration-test-nonsuspension.md`): FT₃ proved over every homotopical 3-computad base** (Γ and Γ₂ homotopical, Cat-valued free fibres), including item 8's ζ: the FT₃ data lift along right whiskering without inverses (λ_{α▷r,e} = (p_e ∗ η^r_{a_e}) · (λ_{α,e} ∗ r)); FT_n with Cat fibres over homotopical bases sketched. Bubble-free ⇒ homotopical is a strengthening of Guetta's open Conjecture 6.6.5 (the item-17 argument gives that conjecture's "only if" half); reformulated as a collapse over the positive computad Γ⁺; conjectured for the decidable one-sided class (all targets nonempty or all sources nonempty), which covers positive, operadic and Q. Open: independent check of the FT₃ proof, FT_n with fibres of dimension ≥ 2, one-sided ⇒ homotopical, bubble-free ⇒ homotopical. Round 5 (verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/finite-fibration-test-higher-base-r5-2026-11-18.md`): an explicit unlocalised attaching map for item 8's ζ by outer-cylinder subdivision (independent confirmation of FTX's FT₃ for that base); mixed localisation 𝓡_A(X) for 2-category fibres; Cat fibres over every 3-computad base with homotopical 2-skeleton and homotopy-pushout 3-cell attachment; suspension bases with 2-category fibres (dimension ≤ n + 2); adjoining 1-generators preserves homotopicality, giving a decidable partition test for homotopical 2-computads; wedge hom attachments with incoming/outgoing arrows and endpoint endomorphisms. Open: ζ and 3-dim bases with 2-category fibres, FT_{n,m}, bubble-free ⇒ homotopical, one-sided ⇒ homotopical beyond the partition test, positive 3-computads. Round-6 response (verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/finite-fibration-test-higher-base-r6-2026-11-18.md`): item 8's ζ with 2-category fibres (triple cylinder W_z, generators χ_e, χ_f, χ_ρ of dimension ≤ 5); general mixed-localisation lemma for finite homotopical X, Y; FT_{3,m} for 3-dimensional bases under item 23's hypotheses (dimension ≤ m + 3); FT_{n,m} for suspension bases. Open: general n ≥ 4 (compatible substitution of higher boundary expressions), bubble-free ⇒ homotopical, one-sided 2-computads beyond the partition test, positive 3-computads. No incremental round-7 prompt; the open items go to the consolidated prompts. **Responses of 2026-11-19, verified:** (i) `conjectures/answered/fibration-presentations-characterization-r2-2026-11-19.md`: every pair (Γ, T) has a presentation of dimension ≤ n + m, and n + m is optimal (Γ = ∂D_{n+1}, Π = ∂D_{m+1}); a uniform presentation Ω keeps the fibre polygraphs and fibre vertices, with auxiliary face copies and explicit localised comparison cylinders; coherent weights and localised values covered; the unaugmented presentation is positively semi-decidable. This meets B14's practical goal and supersedes the compactness/inverse-data-depth items above. Open: a terminating recognition criterion for the unaugmented presentation, follow-up `conjectures/answered/fibration-presentations-characterization-r3-2026-11-19.md` (unsent). (ii) `conjectures/answered/homotopy-polygraphs-2026-11-19.md`: **a finite 2-polygraph is homotopical iff bubble-free** (Heidemann's mesh-model inputs taken on trust; tier-complex core machine-checked by `tools/tier_complex_check.py`), so homotopicality of 2-dimensional bases is exactly characterised and undecidable in general; Theorems A–C give a finite-certificate system in every dimension. Every finite homotopy polygraph is bubble-free in every dimension, including over positive-dimensional cells ([higher-bubble obstruction](conjectures/answered/higher-bubble-obstruction-2026-11-19.md)). The researcher response in `conjectures/answered/homotopy-polygraphs-r2-2026-11-19.md` proves sufficiency for positive-to-one polygraphs and their dimensionwise duals. Sufficiency for arbitrary bubble-free polygraphs is open (owner HPOL, row B24). **Done (FTX, 2026-11-19):** the n + m bound and Ω are in `limits-metatheory.md` §9.5, and Ω's shape with its §8 certificate is the recommended checker rule (inverse data is the special case). Still open: a terminating recognition criterion for the unaugmented presentation ; r3 answered 2026-11-19 (`conjectures/answered/fibration-presentations-characterization-r3-2026-11-19.md`, verified): **well defined ⇒ correct** (actual globular Gray attaching maps suffice; gaunt totals, rigid marked factors, comparison after base change to E_{k−1}), so the certificate is the attaching expressions plus a Gray-boundary check. Open: decidability of existence of the expressions and a base-only criterion, follow-up `conjectures/answered/fibration-presentations-characterization-r4-2026-11-20.md`, **answered 2026-11-20, verified**: Question 3 is proved (Lemma Loc-total, so Proposition W holds over every finite base). Questions 1–2 are partial: terminating factor searches in degree 2 and, under path hypotheses, in degree 3; reduction to connected fibres and to retained base components of dimension ≥ 4; identity-transformation weights work in every dimension; universal goodness glues along vertices. The checker re-runs. **r4 Responses 8–20 verified 2026-11-20:** the universal question U(Γ) is undecidable; one weight is decidable for graph fibres over bases of dimension ≤ 4, for positive finite strict 2-fibres in base dimension 4, and strictly in every dimension for finite strict 2-fibres; two subproblems are r.e.-complete. Responses 21–29 (verified): graded fibres, no raw bubbles, simply connected comparison stages suffice; the universal reduction cannot settle the single-weight problem at fixed bases. Responses 30–31 (verified): weak 4-cells at the nullary circuit computed exactly; the fixed-face double-identity case is decidable by a linear count (fibre dimension ≤ 2); no rule change. Next: `conjectures/retired/fibration-presentations-characterization-r5.md` (retired unsent 2026-11-20: no rule or construction depends on it). **2026-11-20 (B25):** bases are read weakly; Proposition W gives presentations of dim ≤ n + m over every finite base (Lemma Loc-total, r4 Question 3, proved 2026-11-20), `design-decisions.md` §2.5. | FTX: in progress |

Round 5 (verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/cone-over-coherences-r5-2026-11-18.md`): **3-generator extension theorem**, RC(3)(Λ + any 3-generators) ⇔ RC(3)(Λ) for every context Λ of dimension ≤ 2 with an acyclic 1-graph, with no condition on order, whiskering or composability (3-terms modulo 4-terms form a category; a comparison lemma from RC(2) in a fresh 2-variable plus depth-1 naturality; strict 3-cells presented by elementary steps modulo replacement and exchange). | B24 | **Homotopy polygraphs: which finite polygraphs are homotopical?** (originally: homotopical ⟺ bubble-free in every dimension) (finite Γ; no non-identity n-cell 1^{n−1−k}_c ⇒ 1^{n−1−k}_c). Implies an all-dimensional form of Guetta Conj. 6.6.5. (a) **Necessity: proved in every dimension, all k** (HPOL 2026-11-19, independently of the researcher's proof; `homotopy-polygraphs.md` §1, Theorem N: counting functor to Bⁿℤ, section from Σ^k B^q ℕ, Steenrod classes θ_j ι in unboundedly many mod-p degrees; inputs I1–I3 stated). (b) Sufficiency: dimension 2 proved (researcher, verified). **False from dimension 3** (2026-11-20, relative-coherence r6 Response 3, verified): a positive, bubble-free 3-polygraph (A : α₀g ⇛ α₁g, B : gβ₀ ⇛ gβ₁) has a circle fibre; obstructions are idle overlaps / seam rings (fibres S^{k−1} for all k); positive 3-polygraphs without matching idle overlaps are homotopical (Responses 4–5); `homotopy-polygraphs.md` §0. Round 3 (`conjectures/answered/homotopy-polygraphs-r3-2026-11-20.md`, answered 2026-11-20, verified, partial): **exact criterion in dimension 3**: homotopical iff bubble-free and every finite last-step complex L(c) (strict factorizations c = [Q] ∘ p by charts) is contractible; **every dimension**: exact criterion in terms of strict cells (iterated necklace formula, Π⁰₂, decidable with the jump of a bubble-freeness oracle); **positive polygraphs, every dimension**: sufficient "simplex of final choices" condition, recovering positive-to-one and the no-matching case. Round 4 (`conjectures/answered/homotopy-polygraphs-r4-2026-11-20.md`, verified 2026-11-20, partial): **no finite generator-level criterion exists**: homotopicality is undecidable (co-r.e.-complete) on positive 3-polygraphs with five 3-generators (a passive strip in the two-seam ring encodes semi-Thue reachability; erasure lemma), and in each dimension n ≥ 3 by suspension; 0′-hard, at most 0″. **The naive last-step criterion fails in dimension 4** (positive 4-polygraph with every L(c) a point and an S¹ fibre inside one marked step), so an exact criterion must test the marked lower presentations. Both checkers and the Agda certificate re-run. Open: the exact recursive criterion in every dimension, the oracle strength, decidable classes (prompt `conjectures/answered/homotopy-polygraphs-r5-2026-11-20.md`, **answered 2026-11-20, verified**: an exact last-step criterion in every dimension, with coefficient spaces E(σ) and their face maps (Response 1). Homotopicality is Σ⁰₁-hard (finite-strict-cell rank-10 family) and Π⁰₁-hard, and every set truth-table reducible to 0′ many-one reduces to it, with a Π⁰₂ upper bound. Degree 0′ at bounded top weight. Decidable classes: finite strict cells with abelian tests, top weight ≤ 2, graph assembly, one-resource semaphores (homotopical ⟺ ≤ 2 processes admit an excursion), two-resource rings without excursions, and finite test lists for reversal-bounded controls. All 17 checkers and 2 Agda certificates re-run. Round 6 (`conjectures/answered/homotopy-polygraphs-r6-2026-11-20.md`, eight responses, **verified 2026-11-20**, with r5 Responses 16–18): Π⁰₂-complete on a decidable family of supplied poles in one fixed rank-six V (positive typing cannot enforce the selection); Σ⁰₁-complete on supplied boards via a forcing request tree; four generator-level classes closed under suspension and graph assembly: single-transfer (fails iff a strong component has edges ≥ vertices + 2; fibres are products of spheres; coNP-complete, FPT in resources, certificates of R + 2 processes), one-destination (coNP-complete), resource-monotone (decidable by integral homology alone although single fibres are r.e.-complete), binary plus bounded milestones (truth-table reducible to 0′; a fixed milestone table's attainability is r.e.-complete). All 10 checkers re-run. Exact degree still open. Next: `conjectures/outbox/homotopy-polygraphs-r7.md`, unsent: the exact degree; one class containing the four; the fibres beyond sphere products). In-house: mesh-model plan (§3); dimension-3 layerings machine-checked (`tools/layering3_check.py`, §4): the finite layering poset without interchange slices is contractible in every non-empty case tested (~340 random diagrams, all step kinds), but the true fibre includes interchange slices and is an infinite category; the 3-tier sequences of a diagram form the cells of the braid fan inside an open convex cone (checked on 550 diagrams after a model fix), so Π_G is contractible (§4); open: write the pairwise-obstruction step as a proof and reduce the interchange-slice fibre to Π_G (homotopy colimit over 3-tier sequences). Heidemann's thesis gives free (∞,n)-categories on diagrammatic computads for all n (§4.4.3), so sufficiency reduces to (A_n) cell attachment and (B_n) the fibre statement (§5). Follow-up prompt `conjectures/answered/homotopy-polygraphs-r2-2026-11-19.md` was sent and answered (two responses in that file): necessity in every dimension (agrees with Theorem N; also `conjectures/answered/higher-bubble-obstruction-2026-11-19.md`), **sufficiency for positive-to-one polygraphs and their dimensionwise duals** (Zawadowski + Chanavat Cor. 3.27), and groupoidification/homological coherence fail as recognition tests from dimension 3 (Π = (x; a : 1 ⇒ 1; ε : a ⇛ 1)). RCX's independent check (`relative-coherence.md` §7.14) agrees with the verification with one citation-level qualification. Campion's cocartesianness of Sd(µ) → Sd⊳_{[0,k−2]}(µ) is cited to Lemma 4.12, which literally covers only the top cut, and Response 15's cocartesian gluing rule needs it. Chanavat (arXiv 2603.22242, Lemma 2.50 and Cor. 2.51) proves this cocartesianness for frame-acyclic molecules, so the gap narrows to identifying strong-Steiner cells with that setting. It was written up as `conjectures/outbox/sd-cocartesian.md` (since retired unsent), and RCX keeps working on it in-house. **RCX in-house (2026-11-21, `relative-coherence.md` §7.15, sketch): the step closes in Campion's own torsion-free setting.** Chanavat's proof of Lemma 2.50 needs only Campion's Prop. 4.15 at the same k (whose hypothesis holds there), plus a heredity lemma: At_i of a piece glued along dimensions ≤ k − 2 is a union of components of At_i(µ). **Independent check 2026-11-21 (reviewer, against the sources of arXiv 2311.00200 v1 and 2603.22242): the step holds at argument level, at the same level of rigour as Campion's own Lemma 4.12.** *Translation.* Chanavat's Lemma 2.50 cuts [0,k]→[0,k−1] at k = frdim U, which in Campion's indexing is [0,k−1]→[0,k−2]. It uses three things: Prop. 2.18 at that level; the pushout û with Lemma 2.49; and backward induction over the canonical r-pre-layering of u for r < k, where heredity is "maximal elements of dim > k in a piece are maximal in U". §7.15 replaces these with Prop. 4.15 at the same k and the At heredity lemma, and the indices match. *Heredity lemma: holds.* For (a), cofaces of elements of ν of dimension > j stay in ν, because cells are downward closed and ν ∩ ν′ has dimension ≤ j. Then (b), (c) and (d) follow from Campion's Def. 1.11: the equivalence uses atoms c ∈ closure{µ} with a ∈ ∂_i c, and ◁_{i−1} shares an (i−1)-element. So the generating graph of At_i(ν) is the induced subgraph on a union of components, and the generated preorder is the restriction. *Flagged point 2 (iterated gluings): holds.* Every generator, and every canonical-r-pre-layering piece, of a Θ_{[0,k−2]} shape is reached by a binary ∘_j tree with all j ≤ k−2. Property (a) composes along that tree: a coface in µ of x ∈ ν with dim x > k−2 lies in each successive composite, hence in ν. Iterating (b) gives (∂_iν)_i ⊆ (∂_iµ)_i for i ≥ k. *Flagged point 1 (û and Lemma 2.49(1) in Campion's Sd⊳): holds, with an inherited caveat.* An element over θ ∈ Sd⊳_{[0,k−2]} amounts to θ together with an Sd⊳_{{k−1}}-datum on each generator. The data on different generators are independent, because they meet only in dimension ≤ k−2 and the ∘_{k−1}-refinements add only dimensions ≥ k−1. Monotonicity is immediate: restrictions to finer generators refine. This is exactly the unproved step Campion's own Lemma 4.12 takes ("the data of ζ consists of the data of θ along with …"). The extension therefore inherits it, together with the Θ-regularity needed to make the glued θ̂ a subcategory, but adds nothing new. *Residual dependence, not new.* The conclusion rests on Prop. 4.15, as Campion's Thm 4.21 already does. In the v1 source the proof of its input Lemma 4.14 ends mid-sentence ("Thus by induction on m − l,") and carries commented-out \problematic notes. The induction it gestures at is routine, but it is not written out there. **Verdict:** the U_{k−2} gap is closed at argument level, given Campion's §4 machinery. The outbox prompt `sd-cocartesian.md` can be retired (Question 1 answered; Question 2: cocartesian for cuts j ≥ k−2, per §7.15). Retiring it is the owner's call. **Retired unsent by RCX, 2026-11-21** (`conjectures/retired/sd-cocartesian.md`). | C3 | **Decomposition of diagrams**: Diag((Γ, x : A), C) ≃ Diag(Γ, C) ×_{Diag(∂D, C)} Diag(D, C) at every dimension. **Proved** as an isomorphism onto the strict pullback at every k, given the cylinder contexts (unconditional for globular Γ): `free-objects-proofs.md` Thm 3. Strict ≃ pseudo pullback proved at level 0 (Lemma 4) and in the strict model at all levels. Remaining: (C3a) compatibility with Diag's composition, once D3 fixes it; (C3b) boundary restriction is an ω-equifibration at all levels, syntactically. **Lifting (L) done** (2026-11-20, `free-objects-proofs.md` §2.2a): unconditional at level 0, at argument level for n + k ≤ 3, and given C1 on Dⁿ ⊗ Dᵏ beyond that. **Invertibility of the lift (I) depends on C3a** (Diag's composition), and then on (PI), to be built by one simultaneous recursion (inverse, comparisons, unit and counit) per the check below; not yet written out. True in the strict model (Ara–Lucas). The check's points (1)–(3) are applied in §2.2a; (C3c) higher-depth cylinders = C1. **Independent check of §2.2a, 2026-11-20 (reviewer, argument level): (L) holds, but (I)/(PI) has a dependency on C3a that is not stated, and the (PI) sketch needs restructuring.** (1) *(I) presupposes Diag's composition.* "φ̃ is invertible in Diag(Dⁿ, C)" needs a composite ψ ∘ φ̃ and unit and counit cells into identities, so it needs the vertical composition of Diag. §2.3 records that composition as undefined (C3a, blocked on D3). So (I), even at level 0 (a 1-cell of Diag is a lax square, and its inverse needs the vertical composite of lax squares), is not unconditional. Only (L) at level 0 is. Either make C3b depend explicitly on C3a, or define invertibility in Diag componentwise for now. With the second option, (PI) becomes a definition, and its agreement with composition-invertibility moves into C3a. (2) *The inverse is not "x↑ inverted, x± swapped".* ψ's component at x must have type a^ψ_x → b^ψ_x. Both endpoints are fixed: they are x⁺ and x⁻ composed with ψ's *own* lower components. x↑⁻¹ : b_x → a_x has a different boundary, built from φ̃'s lower components. The right tool is Lemma 4 with both endpoints prescribed, not Lemma T, which leaves x⁺ free. Whisker x↑⁻¹ by the inverses of the lower components, then transport it along an invertible cylinder from the naive boundary to the required one. Building that cylinder needs invertible comparison cells between ψ's lower components and the naive inverses. So ψ and these comparison cells, and likewise the unit and counit, whose endpoints are fixed too, must be produced by one simultaneous recursion, not by the sequential "inverse, then unit and counit" order the sketch gives. This is the bookkeeping point, and with this restructuring I see no obstruction. (3) *Lemma T, minor.* Once x⁺ is transported to its prescribed boundary, x↑ : a_x → b_x must include the transport's own cylinder cell from Lemma 4, not only associators, counits and unitors. (4) *Strict model: holds.* Ara–Lucas make the folk model structure monoidal for the Gray tensor, so the restriction from the lax hom out of Dⁿ to the lax hom out of ∂Dⁿ is a folk fibration (every C is fibrant). By the Lafont–Métayer–Worytkiewicz characterization, folk fibrations lift reversible cells exactly, at every dimension. That is the ω-equifibration property. (5) The n + k ≤ 3 bound follows from the dimension count given NGC Theorem A, which I did not re-check. | `free-objects.md` §3.3, §7.2.1; `free-objects-proofs.md` §2 | in progress (C3a adjacent work, C3b) | FOBJ |
| C5 | **Conservativity**: cells of ⟦Γ⟧ between CaTT terms are exactly CaTT terms. Non-collapse half **proved under P** in the strict cartesian model (`free-objects-proofs.md` Prop. 5) and **under L** in the strict Gray model 𝔖 (Prop. 5L, 2026-11-20, argument level: a derivable closed cell t[η] → u[η] gives a cell ⟦t⟧ → ⟦u⟧ in F_s(Γ), for Γ with an ordered globular presentation; equality when Γ has no generator one dimension up). 𝔖 also interprets the biclosed kernel (Theorem S, §5.4). Weak statement **reduced** to BMS + DFMRV (known) and a sound weak interpretation (E11). Remaining: (C5a) weak half, blocked on E11; (C5b) formal side: Theorem S in Agda, which needs Gray tensors and closures of arbitrary strict ω-categories and the comparison κ_Θ : ν(K(Θ)) → ν(λΘ) (paper side done). **Independent paper check 2026-11-20 (reviewer, argument level): §5.4 holds, with three wording fixes.** (a) *κ_Θ.* ν(K(Θ)) is Gray-enriched, not a strict ω-category, so κ_Θ is a Gray-functor into ν(λΘ) viewed as a Gray-category by change of base along the canonical comparison X ⊗ Y → X × Y. That comparison is monoidal: maps into a product are determined by their projections, and the projections X ⊗ Y → X are induced by Y → 1. Compatibility with composition and units follows. κ_Θ preserves boundaries. It sends Gray cells with two or more positive-dimensional factors to identities, because the tuple's dimension max(dᵢ) is less than Σdᵢ. (b) *Marks.* (MARK), (COH-inv), (OP-inv), (INV-fac), (INV-obj), (PS-fac) and (OBJ-k) are each sound as stated. The ps rules of `Gray/Marks` ((OP-ps), (INV⇒PS), (PS*)) are not mentioned. No inv conclusion depends on ps semantically, since the image under (PS-fac) is an identity in any case, so ps can be interpreted vacuously. Say so. (c) *Lemma S1.* It holds for all five rows. State it as naturality in every map of every tensor factor, not only restriction along time-slot faces. Then r is an isomorphism of hom ω-categories, so r⁻¹ preserves composition and strict invertibility. Theorem S needs that for the inv-mode (GB-cut), where c = r⁻¹(b̂) must be invertible. **Formal side 2026-11-21 (thread "Formalize the strict Gray model in Agda"): Theorem S, generic part, is an Agda theorem** (`Gray/Biclosed/Soundness.agda`, `--safe`, no postulates). `Model.agda` states what a semantics needs: a planar multicategory over the formulas (one-place composition with Markl's laws, up to ≈, with an extrinsic typing judgement), invertible precomposition with the corollas m, p, pt, invertible left and right evaluation rows, and globes δ^± , σ with σδ = 1. `Sem.agda` interprets every term from values of the context variables (not from its typing derivation), and every cell type A as a predicate Mem A. `soundness`: if the values satisfy Γ and `infer Γ t ≡ just A`, then ⟦t⟧ ∈ Mem A. Mode inv is read as **flat** (an inv cell has equivalent source and target), which holds when collars are identities; the strictly-invertible reading (the reviewer's point (c)) is not formalized. What the flat reading excludes: soundness is proved only for valuations whose inv hypotheses are flat, and ⟦glue a x b⟧ = ⟦x⟧ relies on that, so a Γ whose invertible hypotheses are valued as non-identity invertible cells is not covered. Scope: the formal Theorem S covers the biclosed fragment (`Gray/Biclosed` syntax) only. That syntax has no L-core coh terms and no marking rules ((MARK), (COH-inv), (OP-inv), (INV-fac), (INV-obj), (PS-fac), (OBJ-k), ps); those cases of Theorem S remain at paper level (§5.4). `PosetModel.agda` is a nontrivial model (finite posets and monotone maps, ⟦Dᵏ⟧ the chain 0 < … < 2k), so the theorem is not vacuous; it gives D7g (below). Plan for the instance: `gray-model-formal-plan.md` (Day convolution over Steiner complexes, 𝔖̂, which contains 𝔖 as nerves; the literal setoid route stalls on the Gray tensor's associativity theorem). **Instance 𝔖̂ done in Agda (2026-11-22):** `Steiner/DayModel.dayModel : Model` (`--safe`, no postulates), the Day convolution multicategory of setoid presheaves on Steiner codes, for any valuation of the object variables (`gray-model-formal-plan.md`, Status). So Theorem S holds in 𝔖̂. Tensors match 𝔖 only as inputs, so this carries over to 𝔖 for judgements whose output is not a tensor, which covers C5b's and D7g's uses; the comparison with 𝔖 by nerves is citation level, and needs every named basis (`nmFB`) to be strong Steiner, which the Agda does not assert (ledger C5d). The literal 𝔖 is C5c. Remaining for C5b: the strictly-invertible reading of inv. | `free-objects.md` §2.6, §5, §7.2.3; `free-objects-proofs.md` §5 | C5a blocked on E11; C5b: generic Theorem S, a poset model and the instance 𝔖̂ done in Agda (inv read as flat); strictly-invertible inv open | FOBJ (C5a, paper side); thread "Formalize the strict Gray model in Agda" (C5b formal side) |
| C5c | **Literal 𝔖 in Agda.** Theorem S with every formula valued as a strict ω-category and ⊗ as the Gray tensor of arbitrary strict ω-categories, maps *into* tensors included. The Agda instance 𝔖̂ (`gray-model-formal-plan.md`: Day convolution of setoid presheaves on Steiner complexes) contains 𝔖 as nerves and agrees with it on closures, representables and every multimap set (N A₁, …, N Aₙ ▸ N Z), so it covers judgements whose output is not a tensor. It does **not** establish soundness for judgements whose output is a tensor, since the Day convolution of nerves is not a nerve. The literal version needs, in Agda: setoid strict ω-categories, the Gray tensor as a term model, and representability of lax multilinear maps in context (the Gray tensor's associativity theorem; Crans 1995, Ara–Maltsiniotis 1607.00668 App. A). The nerve facts used to compare 𝔖̂ with 𝔖 (ν fully faithful on strong Steiner complexes, density) are citation level. | `gray-model-formal-plan.md`; `free-objects-proofs.md` §5.4 | open; not needed for C5b or D7g as stated | thread "Formalize the strict Gray model in Agda" |
| C5d | **Strong Steiner named bases.** The transfer from 𝔖̂ to 𝔖 (`gray-model-formal-plan.md`, Relation to 𝔖) needs every code to be a strong Steiner complex. Globes and their tensors are. The named bases are the free parameter `nmFB : Nat → FB` and can be arbitrary. They cannot be proved strong Steiner in general. The plan: state loop-freeness and unitality as a predicate on a finite basis, give a check for it (bases are finite), and prove that globes satisfy it and that it is closed under tensor (Steiner 2004 Thm 5.11). The transfer statement then takes it as a hypothesis on `nmFB`. **2026-11-23: the predicate and a sound check for it are in Agda** (`Steiner/StrongSteiner.agda`, `--safe`, no postulates). `SS.strongSteiner? B` checks degrees, disjoint ∂⁺/∂⁻ supports, ∂∂ = 0, augmentation, unitality and strong loop-freeness over the finite basis. `SS.sound` turns a `tt` certificate into the pointwise conditions and a height strictly increasing along <₁, and `acyclic` follows from the height: no nonempty <₁-path returns to its start. So a concrete named basis that passes is certified by `tt`. **Correction 2026-11-23:** the first version (PR #493) iterated ∂^α linearly (Σ c(x)·∂^αx) for unitality, which is not Steiner's ∂^α. Steiner takes the positive or negative part of ∂c, with cancellation. The linear version miscounts the 2-simplex: ε⟨x⟩⁻₀ = 2, because the middle point is counted twice. `bdC` now takes the parts, `simplex-ok` checks that the 2-simplex passes, and `GlobeSteiner` is updated (on a single cell the two agree). The check is sound (certificate ⇒ strong Steiner). Completeness is not proved: nothing shows the computed height increases along <₁ on every loop-free basis. Checked: globes λDⁿ for n ≤ 4 (`globes`). A two-point loop basis is rejected (`loop-rejected`) while passing every other condition (`loop-rest`). Globes of every dimension: proved (C5e). Closure under the tensor: proved (C5f, `all-codes-SS`). So the transfer hypothesis reduces to: every named basis is strong Steiner, which a concrete basis certifies with `strongSteiner? = tt`. | `gray-model-formal-plan.md`; `Steiner/Codes.agda`; `Steiner/StrongSteiner.agda` | predicate, sound check (completeness not proved), all globes and tensor closure done; the named bases remain a hypothesis | thread "Formalize the strict Gray model in Agda" |
| C5e | **Globes are strong Steiner in every dimension.** `Steiner/StrongSteiner.globes` checks λDⁿ by computation for n ≤ 4 only. To prove: `SS.StrongSteiner (globeFB n)` for all n. Each boundary of a globe cell is a single cell, so every condition reduces to a lemma about `gb`. The height is h(sᵢ) = i, h(top) = n, h(tᵢ) = 2n − i. **Done 2026-11-23** (`Steiner/GlobeSteiner.agda`, `--safe`, no postulates): `globes-all : ∀ n → SS.StrongSteiner (globeFB n)`. Every condition reduces to facts about `gb`, the single boundary cell of a globe cell: `gb-deg`, `gb-pos`, `gbv` (target and source of one index), `gb-ts`, and the height above (`hA`, `hB`). | `Steiner/StrongSteiner.agda`; `Steiner/Globe.agda`; `Steiner/GlobeSteiner.agda` | done | thread "Formalize the strict Gray model in Agda" |
| C5f | **Strong Steiner is closed under the tensor.** The Gray tensor of strong Steiner complexes is strong Steiner (Steiner 2004; Ara–Maltsiniotis 1607.00668 App. A). **2026-11-23: proved in Agda, no longer cited** (see below). To prove in Agda: for codes K, L whose atomic bases pass `SS.strongSteiner?`, so does the basis of K ++ L with the Leibniz boundary of `Codes`. The unital and loop-free parts are the substance. **2026-11-23, partly done** (`Steiner/TensorSteiner.agda`, `--safe`, no postulates). Loop-freeness: `tensor-height` builds a height for a ∷ K from heights for a and K, H(i, x) = hA(i)·M + s(i, x), where s is hK(x) if deg i is even and max hK − hK(x) if it is odd, because the K-order reverses at odd i. `code-height` extends this to every code. Degrees, disjoint supports and augmentation carry over (`t-degs`, `t-disj`, `t-aug`). ∂∂ = 0 carries over (`t-chain`): the middle sum splits into four blocks (`S-expand`), the outer two cancel by ∂∂ = 0 in a and K, and the mixed two cancel because a nonzero ∂_a(i, k) forces deg i = deg k + 1, which flips the K-sign. Unitality (`Steiner/CellData.agda`, `Steiner/TensorUnital.agda`, `--safe`, no postulates). `CellData B i` records the iterated boundary chains ⟨i⟩^α_p: concentrated in degree p, i at the top, globular (∂⟨i⟩^α_{p+1} = ⟨i⟩⁺_p − ⟨i⟩⁻_p, as an equation in ℕ), with disjoint parts below the top, and ε⟨i⟩^α_0 = 1. From cell data the chains are exactly those computed by `SS.down` (`down-ch`), so the element is unital (`cd-unital`). Every element of an atom satisfying degrees, ∂∂ = 0 and unitality has cell data (`atomCD`). For (i, x) in a ∷ K, `tensorCD` gives cell data ⟨(i, x)⟩^α_k = Σ_{p+q=k} ⟨i⟩^α_p ⊗ ⟨x⟩^{±α}_q, with the K-sign flipping with the degree of the a-factor. Globularity is the telescoping of the Leibniz boundary, and disjointness holds because at each degree one factor has disjoint parts. Result: `code-SS : ∀ K → AllSS K → SS.StrongSteiner (codeFB K)`. Every code whose atoms are strong Steiner is strong Steiner. `all-codes-SS` adds that when every named basis is strong Steiner, every code is (globes by `globes-all`). | `Steiner/StrongSteiner.agda`; `Steiner/Codes.agda`; `Steiner/TensorSteiner.agda`; `Steiner/CellData.agda`; `Steiner/TensorUnital.agda` | done | thread "Formalize the strict Gray model in Agda" |

| D1 | **G(2) uniqueness bookkeeping gap**: check that the cell built from exchange coherences has exactly the type t → t′. Closes Gray generators of dimension 4. | `conjectures/gray-contexts-in-catt.md` §3.3 | closed (proved by Theorem A, NGC 2026-11-18; independently by a researcher, verified 2026-11-18 in `gray-contexts-in-catt.md`) | NGC |
| D2 | **G(n), n ≥ 3, and G-alg**: translate Chanavat's contractible subdivision poset into substituted CaTT coherences (conservativity of molecular coh). Ready to send to a researcher. Researcher response (verified 2026-11-18) confirms G(2) and existence in G(3). It also shows that "(∀n G(n)) ⇒ conservativity" is not established: molecular coherences of dimension above the molecule need a higher filling statement (RC-shaped). | `gray-contexts-in-catt.md` §3.4 and verification §4 | **Narrowed, S9 (2026-11-21).** G(n) holds for every n for every molecule whose context is strong Steiner, which includes every Gray product of globes. Existence follows from RC(n−1) and uniqueness from RC(n) (`relative-coherence.md` §3.3). The higher filling that G-alg needs, namely RC_id(∞) in the molecule's context, holds there too (r8 Response 18). All of it is constructed by structural recursion (r9 Response 15, verified, with Campion's U_{k−2} step closed in `relative-coherence.md` §7.15 and independently checked). Still open: molecules (for example frame-acyclic ones) whose contexts are not strong Steiner. For those, RC is equivalent to homotopicality (r8–r9), and that is the only remaining content of G-alg. **Consolidated 2026-11-18:** G(n) uniqueness/existence for all n is posed as RC(∞) for Steiner loop-free contexts in `conjectures/answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-2026-11-19.md` (answered 2026-11-19; its Q2 asks about frame-acyclic molecules that are not Steiner loop-free). No further per-shape rounds (3 × 3 grid, RC(4) cases). **RC(3) for the lax square fully settled** (researcher round 2 of C1, verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/naturality-arbitrary-depth-r2-2026-11-18.md`: substitution-stable active-column factorisation, normalisers, evaluation fusion; uses that the square has a unique source and sink), so K exists for every 3-dimensional coherence over the single arrow. `conjectures/outbox/gray-contexts-r3.md` (since answered; rounds 4 and 5 answered too, round 6 is `conjectures/outbox/gray-contexts-r6.md`) revised: Q1 is now the Gray cube. existence in G(3) proved (hence Gray generators of dim ≤ 4 typed); G(n≥3) uniqueness and G-alg reduced to RC(n); second researcher response verified 2026-11-18 (`conjectures/answered/gray-contexts-2026-11-18.md`): no solution, but RC(3) for the lax square is settled when the 2-boundary is an identity, coherence 3-terms with boundary F reduce to a vertical chain, the cube's 3-loops reduce to the negative hemisphere, and a total-support form of BM Lemma 36 is proved. Prompt corrections: Chanavat Thm 2.52 needs "not an atom", freeness is Thm 3.17 (not 3.29) and Campion 2311.00200 (not 2311.00205), frame-acyclic ≠ Steiner loop-free. round-2 response (verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/gray-contexts-round2-2026-11-18.md`): F-boundary case of S settled when both 3-terms are literally suspended coherences over vertical chains (via Benjamin–Markakis hom freeness; any context); the shortcut "equal global strict image ⇒ equal hom strict image" is false (explicit κ = r ∗₂ ℓ over D₂); remaining step: reduce F-bearing 3-terms to suspended ones. Round-3 response (verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/gray-contexts-r3-2026-11-18.md`; it answers the pre-revision draft): suspended chain coherences are not a normal form (a hom-word length invariant shows no such composite has boundary F → W(F), already over D₂), so normalisers must include boundary-adjusting terms as in the lax-square proof; the hom-level question is trivially true literally and false recursively. Gray cube, beyond point-supported columns, RC(3)/RC(n) unanswered. Round 4 (researcher, verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/gray-contexts-r4-2026-11-18.md`): **literal, substitution-stable inactive-column factorisation** in every acyclic context of dimension ≤ 2 (inactive columns lift to their path contexts by injectivity; reduced head comparisons, refinement substitutions functorial under substitution, coherence cells between iterated reductions); a coherence 4-term distributing a whisker over vertical composites of 3-cells; on the cube hemisphere at most one active column. Round 5 (researcher, verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/gray-contexts-r5-2026-11-18.md`): **the Gray cube's X-free 3-loops are contractible** (relative evaluation fusion over a formal vertical chain; proper intervals via the two-square cylinders); item 14 extends to 3-dimensional contexts where every 3-generator's 2-source carries a 2-generator (covers the cylinder of D² and c ⋆ D²). **Prompt correction:** an acyclic generating 1-graph does not suffice for RC(3), since (x, a : 1_x → 1_x) is acyclic. Round 6 (verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/gray-contexts-r6-2026-11-18.md`): one-generator extension lemma (RC(3) for Λ plus an isolated 3-generator); **RC(3) for the whole Gray cube, so uniqueness in G(3) for the cube**; RC(3) for the cylinder of D² and for c ⋆ D²; line-word criterion (sketch); local exchange 4-term (24). Open: fusion with exchange (2 × 2 grid), RC(3) in dimension 2 with exchange, several 3-generators, RC(4)/G(4), RC(n). Round 7 (verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/gray-contexts-r7-2026-11-18.md`): **RC(3) for the 2 × 2 grid** (two-square cut fibre via the matrix normal form, flattening lemma isolating the single F/G exchange); vertically composable 3-generators; the cube with an outgoing edge; RC(4) needs 5-terms (31)/(32). The several-3-generator question is settled by the cone round-5 extension theorem (B19), so the RC(3) frontier is general exchange in dimension 2. Open: that, RC(4), RC(n)/G(n). Round-8 response (verified 2026-11-18, `conjectures/answered/gray-contexts-r8-2026-11-18.md`): RC(3) for the 2 × 3 grid (five event words, one square; block-exchange comparison and square 4-cell over D³ ∨₁ D³); conditional 4-dimensional extension theorem (dim Λ ≤ 3, acyclic 1-graph: RC(3)(Λ) and RC(4)(Λ) give RC(4)(Λ + 4-generators)). Open: 3 × 3 grid and general loop-free 2-computads, RC(4) for the triangle, lax square and 4-cube, extension from RC(4) alone, RC(n)/G(n). No incremental round-9 prompt; the open items go to the consolidated RC(n) prompt. | D7c | **Independent check of Lemma GB-ext** (`closed-structure.md` F.7.4 item 4): Response 2's initiality theorem still holds with element generators (probe-only inputs, data output, nullary included), probes as bound arguments of residuals, and the L core in any switch setting V + S as probe base. In-house argument-level proof sketch; the point to check is the interaction of the extra grid tests on pure one-hole homs with the table rows. | `closed-structure.md` F.7.4 §4; `design-decisions.md` §3.3 | **Checked 2026-11-20 (independent reviewer, argument level): (i), (ii) and the conservativity part of (iii) hold up; one gap in (iii).** *The flagged interaction is harmless.* (a) The reflection is a simultaneous localization at a set of maps, so formula (3) holds for every local M whatever the overlap of tests. (b) Where a row relates a pure one-hole hom to a tested one (evaluation rows with Γ pure and a residual output, e.g. O(G; A\C) → O(A, G; C)), the row is a natural-in-G equivalence, so the new grid test there is implied by Response 2's test on the data side: redundant, not conflicting. The new tests add constraints only for pure-input homs with atom or tensor output, and Cat_ω satisfies them (O(P, G, Q; Z) = Map(G, P\Z/Q)). (c) The pure-context pair and unit rows are equivalences between one-hole homs that both carry the new test, so they are consistent. *Step 4 (pure-probe preservation) holds:* a pure-output tree has only pure vertices because no profile has data inputs and pure output, and the point extension at all data-output profiles, now including pure-input ones, is still local. *Step 3 holds:* data-to-probe homs stay empty, and the monoidal identification by the pair and unit rows is unaffected by nonempty probe-to-data homs. *Gap in (iii):* conservativity (no new cells between pasting shapes) uses nothing about the probe base, as the sketch says. But identifying the local objects as data models whose one-hole homs are (∞,ω)-categories, with Cat_ω among them, rests on Response 2's grid tests over the actual Gray probe category and the grid nerve theorem (Keidar–Neuhauser). For a probe base generated by the L core in V + S, this needs either that V + S on grid and globe shapes computes the Gray mapping spaces, or tests pulled back along a comparison U → P. Neither is stated. **Status:** (i), (ii) and conservativity accepted at argument level; (iii) beyond conservativity open. **Gap resolved by restatement (GBIC, 2026-11-20):** (iii) now takes Response 2's own probe base P (the Gray maps of grids and globes), with L's substitutions entering through their interpretation U → P. That is the reviewer's option 2, and it rests on L's soundness in Cat_ω (E11). Conservativity over L is now syntactic: a sort discipline (row outputs are never representables) keeps every derivation of a pure judgement inside L, in every switch setting (`closed-structure.md` F.7.4 §2 and §4 step 4). Option 1 (V + S computes the Gray mapping spaces) is not claimed. **Re-checked 2026-11-20 (independent reviewer, argument level).** *The restated (iii) holds:* taking P as the probe base with L entering through U → P is a faithful use of Response 2, and its dependence on E11 is labelled. Scope: this makes the kernel sound in the initial data model over P. It does not make the kernel's own syntax present that model on pure or mixed homs, since that would need U → P to be fully faithful (option 1). The notes do not claim the stronger statement. *Syntactic conservativity: the core argument holds, but two points need fixing before the claim is accurate.* The core argument is sound. Every rule other than L's substitutions, p and pt has a non-representable output under the sort discipline (rows, m, e^ℓ, e^r, element generators, and the cut and dcut templates, whose goals live over row types). So in a planar tree with representable root, every vertex has representable output, and no data rule enters a pure derivation. (1) *"Exactly L's" overstates it.* The pure fragment is the free planar multicategory on L's substitutions, p_{Δ,Θ} and pt. It is not L itself. With no definitional equations, there are three problems: (a) p ∘ (σ, τ) and (σ ⊗ τ) ∘ p are distinct terms; (b) bracketings of iterated p differ against Δ ⊗ (Θ ⊗ Ξ) versus (Δ ⊗ Θ) ⊗ Ξ; (c) a unary pure term such as p_{Δ,D⁰} ∘ (x, pt) : (⟦Δ⟧ ▸ ⟦Δ ⊗ D⁰⟧) is not an L substitution unless Δ ⊗ D⁰ ≡ Δ and the composite is identified with the identity. Either state conservativity as "no data rule enters a pure derivation; pure judgements are those of L + p + pt", or define pure multimaps (⟦Δ₁⟧, …, ⟦Δₖ⟧ ▸ ⟦Θ⟧) as L substitutions out of Δ₁ ⊗ … ⊗ Δₖ. The second option makes p and pt identities, and needs ⊗ on substitutions together with its strict or L-derivable unit and associativity laws. (2) *Context variables.* The sort discipline restricts row outputs, but nothing stated stops a hypothesis y : (… ▸ ⟦Θ⟧) with a data input in Γ. Such a hypothesis is a profile with data inputs and pure output, which breaks both step 4 and the syntactic argument. The discipline should also restrict context-variable types: pure-output variables only with all-probe inputs, or none at all. **Status:** (i), (ii) and the restated (iii) accepted at argument level. Syntactic conservativity is accepted in the form "no data rule enters a pure derivation", given fix (2). "Exactly L's" needs fix (1). **Both fixes applied (GBIC, 2026-11-20):** (1) the claim is weakened to "no data rule enters a pure derivation; pure judgements are those of L + p + pt", with the reviewer's three examples recorded, and the stronger form (pure multimaps as L substitutions out of Δ₁ ⊗ … ⊗ Δₖ) is stated as not claimed; (2) the sort discipline now also covers context variables: a hypothesis with representable output has only representable inputs (`closed-structure.md` F.7.4 §2, §4 step 4, §5). The generic template contexts satisfy it, since their base profiles H ρ and B ρ have non-representable outputs. **Closed.** | GBIC |

| D7g | **No reverse cells for directed biclosed instances.** In the generic contexts, `no-reverse` and `dir-needs-dir` (`Templates.agda`) are proved syntactically about the template relation. **Proved semantically (2026-11-20, argument level, `free-objects-proofs.md` §5.5)**: for every row, gap and k ≥ 1, no kernel term of any shape has the reverse type u⁺ ⇒ u⁻ in the generic (GB-dcut) context (valuation in the strict Gray model 𝔖 with output the k-globe; chain count). For instances: the reverse is not derivable whenever some valuation in 𝔖 makes σ(u⁻), σ(u⁺) non-reversible (walking square = `Strict/ReverseInterchanger`; four-cube pair of r3 item 41). The earlier wording "unless σ(b) is invertible" was false: σ(u⁻) = σ(u⁺) with σ(b) non-invertible gives the identity as reverse. Remaining: the formal side (Agda interpretation of `Gray/Biclosed` in 𝔖, shared with C5b; four-cube computation in Agda). **Independent paper check 2026-11-20 (reviewer): the theorem holds, but the valuation needs one uniformization.** The probe-pair case is right: b ↦ e ∘ (! ⊗ id), followed by restriction of a reverse term along a point x ⊗ id ⊗ y. The same issue arises in every row, though. Canonical rows (`canonFrom`) allow fixed representables ⟦Θ⟧ anywhere in L, R, Φ, U, V and the evaluation rows' A, and those cannot be valued as 1. Fix: value every object variable except the output as 1, and set b ↦ e ∘ (projection killing every non-slot factor), using the closure bijection when A is representable. Restrict a reverse term along points of all non-slot factors; probes are nonempty. The chain count is unchanged: a k-cell t(e) → s(e) of ν(λDᵏ) would need m·e with m = −1. The lower levels of the tower check out: b^±_j ↦ faces of e, u^±_j ↦ r⁻¹ of them, and a^±_j ↦ identities, so chain(b_j) = b_j and r(u_j) = b_j. The correction of the earlier wording is right. **Formal side 2026-11-21 (thread "Formalize the strict Gray model in Agda"): D7g for the generic contexts is an Agda theorem** (`Gray/Biclosed/D7g.agda`, `--safe`, no postulates). `d7g`: for every row ρ, gap g and k with `okT (tmpl ρ g dir (suc k))`, no term t has `infer (ctx t₀) t ≡ just (reverseTop (cgoal t₀))`. Proof: by Theorem S (C5) in the poset model, with the generic context valued by a tower of monotone maps that ignore every non-slot input (`DirEnv.agda`, which works in any model: β± ↦ r(u±), collars ↦ degenerate cells). A reverse cell x would have x∘δ⁻ = u⁺ and x∘δ⁺ = u⁻; at the interior of ⟦Dᵏ⁺¹⟧ monotonicity gives e(true) ≤ e(false) in the output, which fails because the output of a canonical row is an object variable or a closure into one. This replaces 𝔖's chain count by monotonicity and needs no representable valued as 1. Caveat: it rests on Theorem S with inv read as flat. This does not weaken the generic result, since the generic context's inv hypotheses are degenerate collars, which are flat; it does matter for the instance corollary, which is not yet covered for a Γ whose invertible hypotheses are non-identity cells. **2026-11-22:** the instance criterion is `Soundness.no-term`, in every model (in particular 𝔖̂): one valuation satisfying Γ with no element of the reversed goal type rules out every term of that type. The two examples are computed in 𝒮 in Agda: `Steiner/WalkingSquare.no-reverse-face` (no reverse interchanger) and `Steiner/FourCube.no-uv`/`no-vu` (no 3-cell between 212321 and 123212 either way). u and v are checked to be 2-cells in Agda (`FourCube.uCell`, `vCell`). | D7g-link | **D7g examples as kernel instances.** The walking square and the four-cube pair are proved in 𝒮 (`Steiner/WalkingSquare`, `Steiner/FourCube`), and the criterion `Soundness.no-term` holds in every model. Missing is the link through the syntax. That needs four parts. (1) A context Γ, a directed template t and a substitution σ with Γ ⊢ σ ∶ ctx t, whose images σ(u⁻), σ(u⁺) are meant to be the two paths (resp. u and v). The kernel has no vertical composite of directed cells, so how σ(u±) is built (variables, or `here`/`comp` terms) has to be settled first. (2) A valuation of Γ in 𝔖̂ that satisfies Γ, which means building values of Day-model cell types and checking `Mem`. (3) Yoneda extraction: a cell of a representable-valued operation in 𝔖̂ is a map of 𝒮, with faces given by composition with δ^±. (4) Combining (1)–(3) with `no-term`. **2026-11-23: parts 3 and 4 are done for the walking square** (`Steiner/CellRep.agda`, `--safe`, no postulates). `rep-ext` is part 3: every unary operation (⟦Dⁿ⟧ ▸ y B) of 𝔖̂ represents a map λDⁿ → B, by Yoneda. `rep-face` shows that its faces represent φ ∘ δ^±. `ws-no-term` is part 4: given any valuation ε satisfying Γ in which s and t represent paths with n₀ in s and not in t, no term over Γ has type s ⇒ t in (▸ rep(D¹ ⊠ D¹)). **2026-11-23, design of parts 1–2** (`gray-model-formal-plan.md`, "D7g-link, parts 1–2: design"). The kernel has no vertical composite of directed cells: `comp` whiskers at most one higher cell, and `glue` only adds invertible collars. So σ(u±) can never be the two-edge paths of the square, or the 6-edge paths of the four-cube pair. With variables in their place, the strongest instance is Γ = ctx t with σ = identity, and its reverse goal is already excluded by the generic `D7g.d7g`. Parts 1–2 are therefore not expressible in the current kernel. They become expressible, through `DirEnv` and `CellRep`, if the kernel gains vertical composition. The four-cube analogue of part 4 is open but has the same limit. | `gray-model-formal-plan.md` (Status, D7g-link design); `free-objects-proofs.md` §5.5 | parts 3–4 done for the walking square; parts 1–2 not expressible without vertical composition (reopen if the kernel gains it) | thread "Formalize the strict Gray model in Agda" |
| D8 | ~~Dualities under L~~ **Closed for two-tier L** (`closed-structure.md` §D.1–D.3; Agda `GrayDuality.agda`, reviewed). D_K commutes with ⊗ iff K ∈ {∅, all}, reverses it iff K ∈ {odd, even}; symmetries of two-tier L are J ∈ {∅, {2,3,…}, odd, {1,2,4,…}}, lax↔oplax swaps J ∈ {{1}, all, even, {3,5,…}}, all other J give mixed theories L_ε. **Uniform part done 2026-11-21 (`closed-structure.md` §D.4–D.5):** in V + S, for every setting S, the only symmetry is J = ∅, and the only lax↔oplax duality is J = all, which also exchanges the inner law and R (V + {inner law} ↦ V^∘ + {R}). Proof: the Gray interchanger at every level n (over Σⁿ(ΣD¹ ∨ ΣD¹)) plus the two-tier test; neither alone suffices (J = {1,2,4,5,…} passes the first, op passes the second and reverses the interchanger inside objects). So CaTT's op is not a symmetry of uniform L. Every other J gives a mixed core D_J(V + S), checkable by dualising the input. Biclosed rules: the op/co mirror (⊗ reversed, \ ↔ /, lax ↔ oplax slots) maps the row table to itself, but the probe restrictions need D_K to be a core symmetry, so under uniform L only the identity is a symmetry of L + biclosed (under two-tier L, the identity and the op mirror). Agda: `GrayDualityUniform.agda` (`kernel-theorem`, `dual-canonical`, `dual-has`, `total-mirror`, `level-uniform`) and `Gray/Biclosed/Mirror.agda` (`H-mir`, `B-mir`, `rmap-mir`, `rowsOf-mir`), no postulates. Argued, not machine-checked: the three-flip action of D_J on an interchanger, and that F_{V+S} = 𝔉 on ΣD¹ ∨ ΣD^q (where the two-tier witnesses live; the all-levels argument and its reliance on "everything V adds involves an inner interchanger" and on the S1/S11 reading are in §D.4.1, paper-checked by the reviewer). | E15 | **Mixed `ord` heads.** (COH-ord) is taken only when every object of the pasting context is `ord`. For a head with a subset m of objects marked, the side condition would compute in F_m(Γ) = F_{V+S}(Γ) with the inner letters into marked objects killed, and that is sound by the same factorization. Open: (a) is SC in F_m(Γ) decidable; (b) is the target object alone enough, i.e. is F_{aₙ}(Γ)(a₀, aₙ) = 𝔉(Γ)(a₀, aₙ)? The test case is E13's letter E(Z, b). Progress 2026-11-23: a₀'s mark is irrelevant (no hom into a₀), so (COH-ord) needs only a₁…aₙ (adopted). (b) is expected to be *no*: a binary outer letter whose operand carries inner content from an earlier hom can't be split without binary subdivisions, which are only guarded in V. No countermodel yet (`ord-recovers-two-tier.md` §5). (c) **decided 2026-11-23 (MKL, from the evidence, rule 3/4): keep both**: `iord` (invertible) and `ord` (trivial, with (COH-ord)), `ord` ⇒ `iord`; `iord` is sound in strictly more strict models (E13 loop model) and suffices for (ORD)/(OP-inv_S′) (`ord-recovers-two-tier.md` §6, spec §3.6). | `ord-recovers-two-tier.md` §5 | open; attempt in-house first | MKL |

Design note: `ordinary-math.md` (added 2026-11-18 by OMATH). Rows F1–F6 are its proposals; the lettered rows are what it leaves open.

| F8 | **Exponentials of sets** (function sets ℕ → ℕ, sequences for ℝ): an internal hom restricted to `trunc 0` targets, e.g. Mimram's cartesian closed rules (D12) for sets. | `ordinary-math.md` §6.5 | **proposed and proved sound under P** (2026-11-18, `ordinary-math.md` §6.5.1): a product-parametrised exponential rule (exp, exp-λ, exp-uni at every level) for any A and B, extending `internal-hom.md` §5.4 from representable to arbitrary parameters; `[A, B]` is a set whenever B is (Prop. 6.3, T-exp; semantically `Set(π₀A, B)`); global Π over any base is then a finite limit. Not even κ : A ⊗ W → A × W or a projection out of ⊗ is derivable (Thm 6.4 (a), graded Day countermodel), and the h-level rules do not help. With two new rows of the F.7.4 table, GB-shape (F8a; weakening, and elements = points) and L-× (F8b; contraction at `grpd_2` targets, maximal by Prop. 6.6 (ii)), F8 is derived for every `grpd_2` B with [A, B] := A\B (Prop. 6.5). GB-shape + F8 without L-× does not identify [A, B] with A\B (Thm 6.4 (b)). Verdict by worked comparison: adopt GB-shape + L-×; F8 retires as a primitive under L (kept as the P statement) | FSET |
| F8a | **Rule GB-shape** (weakening; elements versus points). The kernel has no rule relating chapter 7's free objects ⟦Θ⟧♭ (and ⊤ ≃ ⟦D⁰⟧♭) to chapter 8's probes ⟦Θ⟧, so chapter 6's elements ⊤ → C and chapter 8's points ( ▸ C) are unrelated, and ⊗ has no projections (Thm 6.4 (a)). Proposal: corolla ι_Θ : (⟦Θ⟧ ▸ ⟦Θ⟧♭) and the row "shape at slot i". | `ordinary-math.md` §6.5.2 (ii); `spec/09` §9.6, `spec/08` §8.3 | **proposed, sound in Cat_ω** (proved); conservativity over L unchanged (output is data); exactness at argument level (GB-ext step 1). Open: an independent check of the soundness sketches of the countermodels 𝒢 and 𝒬 for every chapter 1–9 rule; whether any existing note silently identifies elements with points. *First-pass audit (2026-11-21):* two places do. (1) `spec/07-free-objects.md` §7.3 and S10 say that the closed structure's ⟦Δ⟧ probes use A1's form, i.e. they read probes as chapter 7's free objects. (2) `internal-hom.md` §5.5 calls a point D⁰ ⊳ B an element, while chapter 6 defines elements as maps ⊤ → B. Both are correct once GB-shape is adopted and are not derivable without it (Thm 6.4 (a)). Full audit (SPEC, ledger S13) adds two more: `closed-structure.md` E.2's B ⊗ Θ with Θ a free object, and `spec/06`'s lax cotensor as a limit against `spec/08`'s C/⟦Θ⟧; all now carry corrections. No other uses were found in the spec or in `free-objects.md`, `limits-representables.md` **Independent check of 𝒢 and 𝒬 (reviewer, 2026-11-21, sketch level): passes.** In 𝒢 every rule that does not mention ⊗, residuals or probes is componentwise, because a product of models is a model when each rule's premises and conclusions are componentwise. The ⊗/probe rules hold because Day convolution is biclosed and restricts to the Gray tensor in degree 0, and the time-slot identification holds because (B ⊗ ⟦Dᵏ⟧)_n = B_n ⊗ Dᵏ. (OBJ-k), EX, Trunc, G and DJ are componentwise. In 𝒬, (y(1) ⊗ y(1)) = y(2) (Day convolution of representables), and Map(y(1), y(2)) = ∅ while Map(y(2), y(2)) = pt. So (L-set) fails, and by the same count no equivalence [A, B] ≃ A\B exists, not merely no κ-induced one. Limits and colimits are pointwise in the enriched functor category, (OBJ-k) holds because a pointwise invertible strict-natural family is invertible, and the ambient homs are ends over ℕ. Caveat: both models read L's ambient as the product, or ℕ-indexed functor category, of the lax ambient of Cat_ω; that reading is the one stated, not re-derived from chapter 1. **Exactness check passed (2026-11-21, argument level, see S13):** through the residual rows, the row family reduces to one condition, that ⟦Θ⟧♭ represents O(⟦Θ⟧; –) on data through ι_Θ. That is locality at a set of maps, and GB-ext steps 3–5 are unaffected. The identification with chapter 7's free object is outside GB-ext's scope. Adoption into the kernel is SPEC's step (S13). | FSET |
| F8b | **Rule L-×** (contraction at `grpd_2` targets): the row H = (A × W, S ▸ B) → B' = (A, W, S ▸ B), r(x) = x ∘ κ. | `ordinary-math.md` §6.5.2 (ii), Prop. 6.6 | **sets: proved sound**; maximality (κ-local ⇒ `grpd_2`): proved; general `grpd_2`: sketch relative to (OBJ-1) and τ₁ preserving products. **Independent check requested** of Prop. 6.6 (ii)–(iii) **Independent check (reviewer, 2026-11-21): (ii) holds; (iii) holds relative to its two cited facts, with two precisions.** (ii): q : D¹ ⊗ Dᵏ → Dᵏ⁺¹ is a chain map (checked on e₁ ⊗ e_k, e₁ ⊗ e_j^± and 0/1 ⊗ x) sending basis to basis or 0, so it is an ω-functor. A (k+1)-cell of D¹ × Dᵏ is an identity because that product has dimension max(1, k) = k. Only essential surjectivity of κ* is used. (iii) needs: (a) (OBJ-1) in the form "the canonical comparison τ₁(A ⊗ W) → τ₁A × τ₁W is an equivalence", since an abstract equivalence would not make τ₁(κ) one; (b) a citation for "Cat_(∞,1) is an exponential ideal of Cat_(∞,ω)" (which by Day's reflection theorem gives τ₁ preserving finite products), currently marked [recall]. The hom-level step (S = ⟦Dᵏ⟧ in the last gap) follows by applying (OBJ-1) to A ⊗ W ⊗ Dᵏ. Follow-up (FSET): (a) is now stated in §6.5.2 (iii). (b) is open: the general question (are the κ-local targets exactly the (∞,1)-categories, and does τ_k preserve products for every k ≤ ω?) was sent as `conjectures/outbox/gray-cartesian-local-targets.md`. **Answered 2026-11-21, verified** (`conjectures/answered/gray-cartesian-local-targets-2026-11-21.md`): the κ-local objects are exactly the (∞,1)-categories, in Campion's inductive and coinductively complete models. τ_k is strong Gray-monoidal and preserves finite products for every k ≤ n ≤ ω (Campion Thm 3.14, Cor. 3.15; Goldthorpe Lemma 3.0.3, extended to ω by generation), so Cat_(∞,k) is an exponential ideal and ⊗₁ = ×. **Done: L-× is sound at every `grpd_2` target** (Prop. 6.6 (iii) proved relative to cited theorems), and maximality now also follows from a retract argument (globe collapse p is a retract of κ_{I,Dʳ}). Correction recorded: the limit along underlying-category functors is the inductive model, not the coinductive one. | FSET |
| F8c | Does K (the kernel without F8, GB-shape and L-×) derive the cartesian F8 by some route unrelated to the biclosed rules? | F10 | **The universe-free rule list keeps growing.** Each thing a universe would give is currently its own rule: R-U (object-valued recursion on rose trees, F5c), EX (effective quotients, F4b), Trunc_n, disjointness of coproducts (T-coprod), G and DJ (families), GB-shape and L-× (F8a/b). Anthony (2026-11-21, spec walkthrough): a growing list of extra rules is "extraordinarily bad"; impredicative definitions without explicit universes (System F) and (co)end calculus are welcome. Goal: a small, fixed set of **general** universe-free principles that derives all of these and closes the list, or a proof that no finite set can. Candidates analysed: (a) descent / van Kampen; (b) inductive fibrations (W-types over a base); (c) impredicative binding quantifiers without a universe (ends ∫_X T(X, X), System F's ∀; lax ends by default; restricted Church-shaped forms ∫_X [[F X, X], X] are initial algebras, so (c) may subsume (b); ends with (−1)-truncated bodies; ends over an object I). | spec walkthrough, piece 0.2; `ordinary-math.md` §4–§6; `spec/09-ordinary-math.md`; **`universe-free-principles.md`** | **in progress** (2026-11-21). Analysis in `universe-free-principles.md`: the universe-shaped rules do two jobs, each covered by one schema. **DESC** (descent for cocartesian fibrations along finite colimits, for all maps along groupoid-indexed colimits, and for truncated families along truncation units) derives EX and T-coprod with disjointness [proved here, sketch], and G from its edge case G₁ [sketch, following Sattler–Wärn]. **INIT** (initial model of any strictly positive finite context; impredicatively, System F's ∀ with the naturality that Awodey–Frey–Speight and Paré–Román show is needed) has Trunc_n, R-A, R-A⁺, ℕ, lists, W-types and R-U as instances. Candidate (b), W-types over a base, is INIT's polynomial case and cannot come from colimits alone (the Adámek tower needs object-valued recursion). GB-shape and L-× are chapter 8 table rows, not universe substitutes (out of scope). Descent fails for arbitrary maps: [agda] `CaTTExp.Descent.PushoutNotUniversal`. **Researcher answer verified 2026-11-21** (`notes/conjectures/answered/universe-free-descent-and-initial-models-2026-11-21.md`): DESC holds along every small colimit; lax gluing holds only along oriented colimits, added as clause DESC-or (gives G₁'s classification half); INIT holds exactly for **limit-preserving targets** (the prompt's colimit-preserving target grammar was wrong: `1 → 2` has no initial model) with **strong** model morphisms (lax ones fail); the end reading is a consequence of initiality, not equivalent. Note corrected accordingly, also T-init (quasi-inverse cells give a non-set). Sub-items F10a–F10h below. | F10 thread |

| F12 | **What does DESC give that INIT doesn't?** (Anthony, 2026-11-23.) Is either of DESC and INIT derivable from the other; which constructions need which; are coinductive types INIT instances? | **`descent-vs-init.md`**; spec §9.2, §9.3 | **answered** (2026-11-23) [proved here, sketch]: neither is derivable. INIT from DESC: F10h. DESC from INIT (and every other non-DESC rule): three locally discrete models where INIT holds by local presentability. In Set, gluing along non-identity automorphisms fails, so ΩS¹ ≃ ℤ and Hom_{⟦L⟧}(x, x) ≃ ℕ are underivable. In the lattice 2, disjointness of coproducts fails. In Pos, universality and EX fail. INIT builds objects by maps out; DESC identifies pullbacks of colimits and fibres of glued families, and its irreducible part is effectiveness (gluing along equivalences). Coinductive objects are not INIT instances: streams ℕ\B are derivable from INIT + chapter 8 (Prop S); M-types use DESC-grpd too (F12b) | F12 thread |

| S1 | **The uniform side condition is not written as a rule anywhere.** `design-decisions.md` §1.1 says derivability is "read off" F_T(Γ), and §1.7 that equality and reachability in F_{V+S} are decidable, but no note states the condition. The spec states it as: F_{V+S}(Γ) has a cell ⟦t⟧ → ⟦u⟧ of full support (coherence case when that cell is an identity), unique by requirement 2. For the strict and Gray quotients this is proved equivalent to the support rule of `pseudo-vs-gray.md` §3.3 (spec Lemma 2.2.1, from `Strict/Recon.b-supp` and `Gray/FullSupp`). Needed: an independent check that this is the intended uniform rule, in particular that full-support reachability rather than a designated total cell is right when F_{V+S}(Γ) has several routes of the same dimension (e.g. ΣD² ∨ ΣD², `joining-completion.md` §5). **Independent check 2026-11-21 (reviewer): the literal reading is not the intended one; read fullness through the Gray projection.** (a) *Literal reading fails.* Spec §2.1 measures supp in the expanded basis and asks for all generators of F_{V+S}(Γ). By (K45)/(K50) of lax r10 §2 that basis records occurrences by position and is infinite already on Γ_{2,2} = Σ[2] ∨ Σ[2] (the powers of H are pairwise distinct; (K59) gives 4-loops on (Σ[1])^{∨8}). So no cell has full support there, and SC_{V+S} rejects even the composite interchanger w = χ(a₁a₂, b₁b₂) and the identity coherence on w, which two-tier L accepts. Even where the basis is finite, the cells uniform L adds above the Gray top cell ((K7)'s χ₁, χ₂; the routes of `joining-completion.md` §5) would all have to occur in c. (b) *What the sources say.* None of `design-decisions.md` §1.1/§1.7, lax r8–r10 or `invertibility-markings.md` states full support in the expanded basis. §1.7 and L4 (r8 Response 1) speak only of a directed cell or an equality existing in F_V(Γ); full support is a Gray-unfolding notion (`pseudo-vs-gray.md` §3.3, §3.6). (c) *Recommended rule.* SC_{V+S}(Γ, t → u) iff F_{V+S}(Γ) has a cell c : ⟦t⟧ → ⟦u⟧ (an identity allowed) whose image π(c) under the requirement-4 projection to 𝔉(Γ) has full Gray support. By Lemma 2.2.1, π(c) is then τ_Γ or an identity on a full-support cell. S2 becomes immediate, and §2.3's examples (a)–(c) keep their stated outcomes. Projecting further to ν(λΓ) is wrong: it would accept a horizontal composite f;g → f′;g′ in Example (a). "Contains the top generator" is ambiguous in V. (d) *Open, needs a SPEC decision.* Uniqueness of c no longer follows from requirement 2, since distinct V-cells can share a Gray image. Examples: H and 1_w on Γ_{2,2}; the coherence case can take the identity. In the operation case, Example (b)'s u ≠ v (settings ∅, {R}) are parallel with the same Gray image (K57), so the strip's own top coherence apparently has two candidate interpretations. ⟦coh⟧ needs either a uniqueness lemma for the lifts used, or a designated lift that is stable under substitution (requirement 5), or acceptance restricted to Γ where the lift is unique. This is the designated-total-cell question above, and it is now confirmed to be real. | `spec/02-lax-core.md` §2.2–2.3 | literal reading refuted; Gray-projection reading recommended (reviewer 2026-11-21). **Adopted 2026-11-21 (SPEC):** `spec/02-lax-core.md` §2.1–2.3 now read fullness through π : F_{V+S}(Γ) → 𝔉(Γ), and the decision procedure filters paths by π-support. (d) decided: the interpretation is a designated lift fixed per coherence head (identity in the coherence case; the first path in the procedure's fixed enumeration otherwise), stable under substitution because heads are; uniqueness up to coherence is S11 | SPEC (restate §2.1–2.3, decide (d)); S3 checker follows the chosen support function |
