Corrections done; exactness check passed (below) **Independent check of the exactness argument (reviewer, 2026-11-21, argument level): passes, so K2 can be adopted.** (1) *Locality at a set of maps.* The left and right rows give O(L, ⟦Θ⟧♭, R; Z) ≃ Map(⟦Θ⟧♭, L\Z/R) and O(L, ⟦Θ⟧, R; Z) ≃ O(⟦Θ⟧; L\Z/R). Probes are allowed as bound arguments (GB-ext (ii)). So the whole row family says that the data atom ⟦Θ⟧♭ represents O(⟦Θ⟧; –) on data objects through ι_Θ. That is locality at one map per Θ (or one per (Θ, L, R), still a set). Step 1 of GB-ext goes through, and Cat_ω is local because ι_Θ = id (soundness). (2) *Steps 3–5 are unaffected.* ι_Θ is probe→data, an element generator in the sense of GB-ext (i). Step 3's picture (probe→data homs may be nonempty, data→probe homs empty) is respected. Step 4 is unchanged because no profile has a data input and a pure output, so the row never enters a pure derivation. Step 5 holds with ι_Θ ∈ Σ. (3) *Precisions, none blocking.* (a) The argument makes ⟦Θ⟧♭ a data atom of the biclosed free model. Its identification with chapter 7's free object (LIFT) is a further consistency, sound in Cat_ω but outside GB-ext's scope, as for every non-biclosed chapter. (b) With Θ = Dᵏ beside a data input, the converted probe is read as a time slot. As stated, the row has no extra slot S, so its GB-dcut instances need S, if wanted, placed as in the other rows. (c) Derived consequence: with the pair and probe-pair rows, ⟦Δ⟧♭ ⊗ ⟦Θ⟧♭ ≃ ⟦Δ ⊠ Θ⟧♭. That is true in Cat_ω and worth recording. | SPEC |
| S14 | **Path length in F_{V+S} is not bounded.** `spec/02` §2.3 and Lemmas J-dec / V-dec (`design-decisions.md` §1.5, §1.7) said the positive gradings (K45)/(K50) bound a path by its endpoints. That is false where free objects have loops: the loop H of V at degree 4 on Γ_{2,2}, and a positional self-loop at degree 5 on the 2 × 2 grid in T₁₂ (`notes/uniform-checker.md` §1.1, found by the S3 thread). Decidability survives, because the vertex set at fixed faces and weight is finite (K58) and r10 Response 3 searches it breadth-first. Fixed in the spec: SC is a breadth-first search over (vertex, accumulated support) pairs, and the designated lift is the first shortest witness. Corrections are also added to both lemmas. The Agda search over pairs is `Uniform/RuleFin.agda` (S3e). **Tension with (K49) settled 2026-11-21 (DSGN):** the T₁₂ self-loop is the cut-3 whiskering [χ(b₂a₁) \| I₂(χ(b₁a₁), χ(b₂a₂)) \| χ(b₁a₂)] of a directed bare square, the suspension of H. It is not a (K49) four-input rectangle, because no temporal cut of the source word b₁b₂a₁a₂ separates the two active inputs. T₁₂ does have both full splits, so the spec's identification of the all-on setting with T₁₂'s profile stands. Nothing downstream changes (`design-decisions.md` §1.7). | `spec/02` §2.2–2.3; `design-decisions.md` §1.5, §1.7 | **fixed 2026-11-21** in spec and notes; Agda search over pairs done 2026-11-21 (`Uniform/RuleFin.agda`, `Uniform/KernelDec.agda`; S3e) | SPEC |
| S15 | **Chapter 0 contradicts itself on internal quantifiers.** `spec/00-principles.md` §0.1 (and project standard 5) allow internal Lawvere-style quantifiers, i.e. a rule that binds an object variable with a universal property witnessed by cells. §0.2 says "an object is never quantified over by a term former", which forbids exactly that. Found in the spec walkthrough (Anthony, 2026-11-21, asking for System F-style impredicativity / (co)ends without universes). Fix: reword §0.2 so that it forbids universes (a type of types, a judgment turned into a term) but not binding quantifiers/ends whose universal property is witnessed by cells; which such quantifiers are sound is F10(c). | `spec/00-principles.md` §0.1–0.2 | **fixed 2026-11-21**: §0.2 now separates what it excludes (classifying objects, reflecting judgments into terms) from what it allows (binding formers witnessed by cells, ranging over the sort ⋆). No other chapter repeated the claim (spec searched). | SPEC |
| S16 | **Marks no longer recover two-tier L from uniform L, so principle 0.4 as written is false.** `spec/00-principles.md` §0.4 says uniform L ⊂ two-tier L ⊂ P, "each being the previous one plus marks". After E13 (PR #398), `ord` on every object recovers two-tier L's `inv`/`ps` judgments for operand-pure heads only, and does **not** recover two-tier L's coherences in any setting (the loop H vs 1_w; the outer W-strip): spec §2.5, §2.6, §3.6. So a user who wants ordinary ω-categories as objects cannot get two-tier behaviour from the uniform kernel by marking. Found in the spec walkthrough (piece 0.4, 2026-11-21). Needed: (a) reword §0.4 to match §2.6; (b) design work: find a mark or rule, in the allowed rule classes, that does recover two-tier coherences, e.g. an `ord` that changes the side condition for heads landing in an `ord` object (computing those homs in the quotient of requirement 4 instead of only inverting inner letters), with soundness; or prove no such mark exists, in which case record what principle 0.4 becomes. | `spec/00-principles.md` §0.4; spec §2.6, §3.6; E13, E14 | (a) **fixed 2026-11-21**: §0.4 now states the ordering by derivability, and says marks recover P from two-tier L but recover two-tier L from uniform L only for `inv`/`ps` on operand-pure heads, not its coherences. The chapter 3 intro was corrected to match. **(a) second pass done with (b) (MKL, 2026-11-21):** §0.4 now says marks recover two-tier L from uniform L, using `ord` with (COH-ord), and that an invertibility-only mark would not. (b) **done 2026-11-21 (MKL)**, `ord-recovers-two-tier.md`: a recovering mark exists. Read `C ord` as "inner letters into C are trivial", and add the rule (COH-ord): a head all of whose objects are sent to `ord` objects is accepted on the two-tier side condition SC_𝔉. This is a `coh` rule over an annotated pasting context, so it is in the allowed classes. It is sound in every strict model with trivial inner letters at its `ord` objects, because the model's map factors through F_{V+S}(Γ)/⟨inner letters⟩ = 𝔉(Γ) (requirement 4). With every object `ord` it gives exactly two-tier L (terms, types, marks). It is decidable. **Agda** `Uniform/OrdCoh.agda` (rule level; path-level factorization). Any mark that only inverts letters cannot do this (E13's loop model). Spec §3.6, §2, §5 updated. Mixed heads → E15. **(a) consistency pass (SPEC, 2026-11-21):** the chapter 3 intro still said `ord` recovers ordinary ω-categories "only in part", and §0.4 listed pseudo transformations and groupoids, without `ord`, as the marks, plus a stray duplicate bullet. Both corrected, so §0.4, the chapter 3 intro, §3.6 and §2.5–2.6 now agree. | (a) SPEC; (b) MKL (done) |
| S18 | **The spec carries history.** `spec/README.md` says the spec records the current answer only and that superseded designs stay in `notes/`, yet several chapters say "an earlier version of this section…", "the first version … was refuted", "corrected under ledger S15" (grep `earlier version|first version|refuted|superseded|corrected under` in `spec/`). Anthony (spec walkthrough, 2026-11-21) asked why historical notes are in the spec. Fix: move every such sentence to the note the rule cites (or the ledger entry), leave the spec stating only the current rule and its status, and add a sentence to `spec/README.md` that change history lives in git and the ledger. | `spec/` (all chapters); `spec/README.md` | **fixed 2026-11-22 (SPEC)**: history removed from chapters 0, 2, 3, 5, 7, 8 and 9. Each passage either became a present-tense justification (a counterexample to a simpler rule) or was dropped where the note or ledger already records it (S1, S10, S13, S14, S15, F1 Thm 4.4 correction, F6a, `uniform-marks.md` §8). `spec/README.md` now says change history lives in git and the ledger. | SPEC |
| S19 | **Surface extensions are trusted by verified conservativity, not by re-checking; first instance: strictified associators and unitors.** Anthony's decisions (spec walkthrough, 2026-11-22): (1) the user-facing language may strictify two of the three kinds of structure (associators and unitors, or another pair) by rewrite rules, and similar rules may be added for other things; only the kernel has no computation rules; (2) surface output is **not** re-checked by the kernel: every layer above the kernel (elaborator, rewrite systems, tactics) is formalized in Agda and proved conservative over the kernel once, and its output is trusted by that proof; (3) everything is formalized, not only the kernel. Spec §0.3/§0.5 to be revised (spec thread). Work needed: (a) state conservativity precisely (every surface derivation elaborates to a kernel derivation of the corresponding judgment, and the surface derives no kernel judgment the kernel does not); (b) prove it for strict associators + unitors over uniform L with directed interchange (the Gray-category-style 2-of-3: interchange stays weak/directed). Literature: CaTT_sua (Finster–Rice–Vicary, Agda-formalised reduction), Gordon–Power–Street (tricategories ≃ Gray-categories), Simpson's conjecture (units weak); whether all-dimension semistrictness of this kind is conservative is open in the literature [conj], so attempt a proof and prepare a researcher prompt (standards 1–2); (c) the strictification theorem in Agda, building on `CaTTExp.SU` (D16). (d) each surface rewrite system's own decidability, i.e. termination and confluence of its rules, formalized with its conservativity proof (spec §0.6 requires decidable checking; CaTT_sua proves this for its rules). | `spec/00-principles.md` §0.3, §0.5; `icatt-and-rice-rules.md` §4; D16; `surface-conservativity.md` | **in progress (2026-11-22, `surface-conservativity.md`).** (a) **done**: inhabitation, elaboration and relative-coherence (EL) forms stated (§2). (b) **reduced and partly proved**: Theorem A (E + SC + kernel conversion KC ⇒ every surface derivation of a kernel type elaborates to a kernel term of that type) **Agda-checked** (`Surface/Conservativity.agda`); Theorem B (KC ⇐ EL in lower dimensions, by whiskering over linear trees, valid in uniform L) proved on paper; in Agda over a whiskering interface (`Surface/TheoremB.agda`, 2026-11-23). For plain CaTT, `Surface/WhiskerPure.agda` (2026-11-23) builds the interface from the derived whiskers, proves its typing rule, and shows the module parameters jointly satisfiable (`Wh-≈` and `Unit` only trivially). **Plain CaTT complete (2026-11-23):** with ≈ generated by image equality in ν(λΓ) on typed terms, every hypothesis of Theorem B (EL ⇒ KC) is discharged, including the unit law `Wh-≈` (strict unitality; `Surface/Generated`, `Surface/Image`, `Surface/WhiskerImage`, `Surface/ImageCatt`); the conservativity corollary there still needs (SC) for this ≈ (part of Lemma 1.3), and the generated ≈ is sound for top chains, so it is not trivial (`sound-top`, `var-distinct`). **Two-tier L complete (2026-11-23):** with ≈ generated by image equality in 𝔉, every hypothesis of Theorem B is discharged too, including `Wh-≈` (`Surface/ImageGray`; 1-cell identities are the empty tuple, higher ones top chain 0; the heads' unit hypotheses carry a dimension premise, `Surface/TowerDim`); (SC) for this ≈, soundness/non-triviality of the generated ≈ for 𝔉 (the analogue of `sound-top`, `var-distinct`), and the agreement of `cohTL` with `Strict/L.towerOf` are not proved. Still open, owned by this thread: the interface for uniform L (history of the two-tier L typing half: two-tier L is reduced to graySide accepting Derived's whisker heads for every k, j: `DerivedWith`, `Surface/WhiskerWith`, 2026-11-23; checked by evaluation for k + j ≤ 3, `Surface/WhiskerGrayCheck`; the heads are suspensions, `Gray/WhiskerSigma`; on one hom isOp gives graySide's operation case for L-derivable sides, `Gray/SingleHomSide`, 2026-11-23; graySide accepts every head with k ≥ 1, `Gray/WhiskerSide`, 2026-11-23; graySide accepts every right whisker head with k = 0, `Gray/WhiskerZero`, `Gray/WhiskerZeroSide`, and every left one, `Gray/WhiskerZeroLeft`, `Gray/WhiskerZeroLeftSide`, 2026-11-23; so graySide accepts every head and WhiskerWith is instantiated at graySide, `Surface/WhiskerGray`: the typing half of the two-tier L interface is proved; uniform L's interface remains); Theorem C (conservativity ⇒ EL) **Agda-checked**; so conservativity ⟺ EL. With ≈ = equality in the side condition's own free objects (strict associators and unitors, interchange still directed), the surface is conservative over two-tier and uniform L **in every pasting context, all dimensions**, modulo Lemma V (argued for two-tier; (ii) open for F_{V+S}). Plain CaTT corollaries: full strictness conservative in strong-Steiner contexts and dim ≤ 3 everywhere, not in (x, a : 1ₓ → 1ₓ) at dim 4; **Catt_sua/Catt_su conservative over Catt in every strong-Steiner context and every context through dim 3** (literature: pasting contexts only). **Open:** arbitrary contexts = Conjecture GRC; paste-ready prompt `conjectures/outbox/semistrict-surface-conservativity.md` (ready to send); insertion vs designated lifts in uniform L (links S11); Lemma V; decidability of ≡_F in arbitrary contexts. (c) **partly done**: reduction and converse in Agda; Theorem B in Agda over the whiskering interface, discharged completely for plain CaTT (`Surface/ImageCatt`, 2026-11-23) and for two-tier L (`Surface/WhiskerGray`, `Surface/ImageGray`, 2026-11-23); uniform L's interface and Lemma 1.1 in Agda still to do. (d) open (found 2026-11-22). All open parts owned by this thread. **2026-11-22 follow-up (review fixes + Anthony's subset question):** ≈ is now context-indexed in the Agda and (KC) is local (Γ plus pasting contexts); `Converse.RelCohAt` carries the ≈ 1 half; dimension-bounded equivalence (Cor. 3.4) on paper. **Interchange can never be strictified over L** (`Surface/StrictInterchange.agda`, Agda: any equality identifying the two layerings over ΣD¹ ∨ ΣD¹ breaks conservativity), so over L only S ⊆ {A, U} remain, all reducing to GRC. Over P: every S conservative in strong-Steiner contexts and dim ≤ 3; {U, I} ⊆ S fails at dim 4 in (x, a : 1ₓ → 1ₓ) (full set proved, {U, I} argued); {A, I} (Simpson) and {A, U} open (Conjecture SC-P). Outbox prompt generalised to the full classification. Lemmas 1.2, 1.3, V are argued, owned here. | spec text: SPEC; (a)–(d): S19 thread |
| S20 | **Components trusted today by re-checking (S19 inventory).** Under S19, every layer above the kernel is proved correct in Agda, and its output is trusted without a re-check. Γ ⊢tm w ∶ arr A t u)` with every answer typed by a proved lemma, no re-check, covering identities, RC(1) through the chain filler, and globular contexts through `fill-total`, with totality proved for all three (`rcP-id`, `rcP-1`, and `rcP-glob` since 2026-11-23); stage 2, support restriction, is done (2026-11-23, `CaTTExp.RCProc.Support`, `RCProc.rcS`: answers on the dependency-closed support, pulled back along a typed inclusion, so every query with globular support is answered by proof; when dim τ ≤ k, every flagged leaf has dimension k (`TopOK`) and some leaf is flagged, dimension k + 1 of the raised context and ∂⁻/∂⁺ of it equal to the images of all of τ under incl∓; for arbitrary flags, incl∓ are variable substitutions, a term using every level of τ has images using every level in the image of incl∓, and every level of the raised context is in one of the two images or is a new cell incl⁻ w → incl⁺ w); the top-slot congruence cell of an operation is typed with its side condition by proof (`CaTTExp.PsRaiseCongr.cong-cell-op`, 2026-11-23); for stage 3c, the term of a 2-path is defined from the data alone and typed (`CaTTExp.RCProc.TwoPathTerm`: `Term.term`, `term-wf`; the whiskered-generator cell `RCProc.Eg.eg-wf`, 2026-11-23; its boundary hypothesis follows from `Key2Ctx`, `RCProc.Key2Bd.key2-bdok`, via the strict image of a 1-cell, `RCProc.StrictPath.top-path`); stage 3d, Lemma G (ii) up to filler cancellation, is proved (`RCProc.LemmaG`: `lemmaG⇒`, `lemmaG⇐` connect H_{m′,n′}[θ′] with κ̃⁻[θ_g] ∗₁ H_g[θ_g] ∗₁ κ̃⁺[θ_g] in both directions, by one coherence over the column context, `RCProc.LemmaGCoh`, 2026-11-23); the rank ≥ 3 reductions, Theorem A and the descent still come from certified tables (stages in `rc-generator.md` §1);  (ii) `GrayCtx.Elab`, an uncertified elaborator (`gray-cylinders.md` §6); (iii) the uniform checker relative to unconstructed layers (S3a). For (iii), the (vertex, support) search is proved sound and complete (`RuleFin`), and since 2026-11-22 so is the normal-form construction for any finite presentation (`Present`); what is still trusted is the presentation of each layer from raw T₁₂ syntax (S3a). Already proved correct and needing nothing: the graph and globular filler tactics (`GraphFillerTotal`, `FillTotal.fill-total`). | `rc-generator.md` §1; `gray-cylinders.md` §6; spec §9.2 | open (found 2026-11-22). **(i) stage 4, blocker (2026-11-23):** Horiz3's query (2), the coherence-only cap-block comparison, is unanswered. The marker step for composite faces is correct and terminating, but it applies only when no generator acts on a proper part of the marked face (otherwise no bundled forms exist), and query (2) violates that (x4 acts inside x10 ∗₀ x2). The fold route (r9 Response 4) recurses into cylinder contexts that grow (35 to 87 variables) and was stopped. The general step is Response 15's descent, which is settled on paper for strong-Steiner contexts (query (2)'s context is one, checked in Python by `steiner.Complex.is_strong_steiner`, not yet in Agda). What is missing is its implementation, `rc-generator.md` §4 steps 4, 5 and 7 (`rc-generator.md` §7). **Update (2026-11-23):** the single-piece step is implemented (`tools/rc_descent.py`). Query (2) lies in one piece and its in-arity comparison answers, but the bridges of its whiskered rank-3 generators need the l-component of Response 15's induction (adapted pieces R_l, M_l, and cylinder staircases as recursive calls), which is not implemented yet (`rc-generator.md` §7). **Update (2026-11-24):** with the bridge calls recursing through the descent itself, the earlier bridge failure is passed; the reassociation-and-unit naturality fillers are now built from pasted elementary squares (`rc_nat._assoc_filler`); the next blocker is a composition head with moving lower-dimensional whiskers (`rc_nat` `NoFiller`, `rc-generator.md` §7). **Update (2026-11-25): query (2) is answered.** `tools/rc_query2.py full` answers it by the descent from an empty cache in 4704 s (1772 oracle answers, 35 single-piece steps), and the answer type-checks as t → u in the query's context (Python checker, also from an empty memo). Unblocked by the oplax transfer and transport-square `strip_lower` fixes and three term-kernel fixes in `catt.py` (`rc-generator.md` §7). What remains of stage 4 is the Agda side: the descent as a procedure proved to return typed terms. **(ii) in progress (GCTX, 2026-11-22).** Plan: prove every `Elab` operation correct once, for all inputs, so no output is re-checked: (1) `fill` (the substitution read off the images of a tree's locally maximal variables) is well typed when the images are typed and agree on shared boundaries; (2) a coh over `ctxOf T` is well typed from (1), `psOf` and the shape's own typing and side condition; (3) the shape facts once per shape: fixed trees (associators, the naturality and functoriality coherences of `VComp`) by computation on the tree, dimension-parametric ones (`wrap k`) by `Suspension`, arity-parametric ones (`vcomp`, `regroup`, `whiskIn`) by induction on 1-dimensional chains, whiskerings by `Derived` (proving `rw`/`lw` equal to `BK`/`LK` for all k, j, today checked only for k + j ≤ 5); (4) the D15 lift engine (`GrayCtx/Lift`) on raised trees, which also gives the D15 typing theorem. **Done (2026-11-22):** (1) `GrayCtx/ElabCorrect/Fill.agda` `fill-ok` (fill returns σ₀ itself for images of a typed σ₀ whose keys cover P); (2) `ElabCorrect/Coh.agda` `cohT-ok` (cohT correct on well-typed inputs, i.e. the images of a typed σ₀ : `ctxOf τ`, given the shape facts `ShapeOK`). The converse soundness direction (`cohT`/`fill` returns `just t` ⇒ t typed, for arbitrary inputs) is not proved and is part of the remaining (ii) work, unless every caller is shown to pass images of a typed σ₀; fixed shapes get `ShapeOK` once, as a closed proof `cert (shape? τ b) tt` checked when the library is checked (not per use; the parametric families instead need inductive or suspension proofs, since running `shape?` at use time would be a per-use check) (`ElabCorrect/Shape.agda`), first instance `ElabCorrect/Assoc.agda` (associators correct for every typed chain). **Done (2026-11-23):** `vcomp` for every dimension k + 1 and every length n ≥ 2 (`ElabCorrect/Chain.agda` for k = 0 by induction on n: the chain tree builds `chainC n`, its tops are the edges, coverage and the side condition proved for all n; `ElabCorrect/Susp.agda` lifts the shape facts through `wrap` by suspension: `ctxOf (node [τ]) ≡ Σctx (ctxOf τ)`, tops shift by two, the builder commutes with Σ, typing by `Σtm-wf`, side by `sideΣ`, coverage by `cover-Σ`; theorem `vcomp-ok k m`). The suspension transfer is stated for the vcomp builder; other families reuse its lemmas. Next: regroup, whiskIn, identities, unitors, `covers` for every tree, `rw`/`lw` equal to `BK`/`LK` for all k, j, and the lift engine (4). | (i) stages 1–3 in Agda (globular totality, support restriction, local reductions, Theorem A): "Prove the RC generator correct in Agda" (from 2026-11-23), except stage 3f (equal source 1-paths and equal bags of 2-variables imply exchange-equivalent 2-paths, the combinatorial core of `rcS-thmA`, needed by C1d's dimension-2 fillers) and then 3e (Lemma E cells and sorting), taken by "Make the relative-coherence construction executable" (2026-11-23, at the coordinator's request; split agreed with the stage 1–3 owner, who keeps 3c, 3d, 3g and the final assembly of `rcS-thmA`). **3f done (2026-11-23):** `CaTTExp.RCProc.TwoPath.TwoPaths.Theorem.exchange-complete`: two 2-paths (whiskered generators with composite 1-path boundaries) with the same source 1-path and the same bag of 2-variables are exchange-equivalent, under the strong order up to dimension 2 (`Key2`); the hypothesis of `rcS-thmA` is `Key2Ctx Γ` (`CaTTExp.RCProc.TwoPathCtx`) in place of `Ordered Γ`, since `Ordered` includes `PsCtx` and excludes the naturality cylinders Q↑Y (2-cells with composite boundaries); some such condition is needed: for g, h : f ⇒ f, the 2-paths g;h and h;g have the same source and 2-variables but are not exchange-equivalent, and Key2Ctx has no instance (`CaTTExp.RCProc.TwoPathFacts`: `gh-not-~`, `no-key2`, 2026-11-23; and no CaTT 3-cell connects g ∗₁ h and h ∗₁ g, by a model argument in a strict ω-category whose 2-cells f ⇒ f are words over Bool: `CaTTExp.RCProc.TwoPathModel.no-3cell`, 2026-11-23). The theorem works from equal source 1-paths and equal bags of 2-variables; turning equal ≈w strict images into those is the strict-image lemma of the stage 1–3 owner (`tups-≋`, `path-unique` help). `Key2Ctx` holds for every pasting context (`TwoPathFacts.key2-ps`, 2026-11-23, by "Make the relative-coherence construction executable", at the coordinator's request). Next: `Key2Ctx` for Q↑Y (owned by the naturality-filler thread; expected on paper, not proved), then 3e. **3e part (1) done (2026-11-23):** `CaTTExp.RCProc.Exchange.TwoDisc` (`xch-wf`, `xch-wf′`): over the two-disc pasting context E (a chain, a 2-disc Z₁, a chain, a 2-disc Z₂, a chain; every length), the two orders of firing Z₁ and Z₂ are typed 2-path terms L and R (`Term.term` with `key2-ps`), both full, so coh E (L ⇒ R) and coh E (R ⇒ L) are typed for every θ : Γ → E. Part (2), relating L[θ] and R[θ] to the 2-path terms of Γ for an exchange step of Γ, uses Lemma G (`RCProc.LemmaG`, on main) and the 3-cell glue `CaTTExp.RCProc.Glue3` (2026-11-23: congruence of vertical composition in either slot, associativity both ways, and `cancel`: parallel 2-cells over x → y with only 0- and 1-dimensional free variables, such as composites of chain fillers, are connected, by `rcS-graphSupp` on their support); **part (2) done (2026-11-23):** `CaTTExp.RCProc.ExchangeGamma.Setup.Main.exchange`: for every exchange step of a context Γ (any Γ, key, boundary lists p∓ with `BdOK`; 2-variables α : s₁ ⇒ t₁ and β : s₂ ⇒ t₂ with walks λ, μ, ρ in Γ), the 2-path terms of the two orders, `Term.term a b P` and `Term.term a b Q`, are connected by 3-cells both ways (`Glue3Eqv.Eqv`), with no hypothesis beyond the data. Proof: pull E back along θ_E : Γ → E (`ExchangeGamma`), compare each whiskered generator's cell with its pullback by a square K⁻ ∗ g ⇄ e ∗ K⁺ (`RCProc.ExchangeSq.Sq.sq`, from Lemma G and filler cancellation), paste the squares (`Glue3Eqv.paste`), apply the exchange cell of part (1), and remove the outer filler (`Glue3Eqv.stripE`, by a new identity 2-cell and unit law, both concrete coherences); the lift to exchange sequences is done too (`CaTTExp.RCProc.ExchangeSeq.Seq.seqE`: for every chain S —(w ∷ P)→ T from a walk a ⇝ b and every Q with (w ∷ P) ~ Q, `term a b (w ∷ P)` ⇄ `term a b Q`, both ways; one step anywhere is `xchE`, the identity case is the new concrete coherence `Glue3Eqv.reflE`), so with 3f's `exchange-complete` both pieces of the 2-path half of `rcS-thmA` are in Agda (no single theorem composing them is stated yet, and `seqE` takes a nonempty source path w ∷ P; to be stated in the final assembly); stage 4 (the descent): "Make the relative-coherence construction executable"; (ii) GCTX; (iii) "Formalize the V + S free objects in every degree" (S3a) |
| S21 | **Evidence layer for saturation.** Proposed in `marking-alternatives.md` §6 / spec §3.7: evidence `!` (the judgment), `inv(t; s, s′, l, r; e_l, e_r)`, `can(coh[γ]; {i ↦ e_i})`. Agda-checked for dimensions 1–2 with conservativity and the two examples (`Gray/MarksSaturation.agda`). Remaining: (a) general-dimension inv-intro in Agda (suspended composite); (b) ~~a Ps-by-data rule~~ **closed 2026-11-23, negatively**: the generic-cell test is exactly invertibility, so pseudo-ness cannot be certified from data and must be declared (`marking-alternatives.md` §8.2, verified answer `conjectures/answered/invertibility-certificates-2026-11-23.md`); (c) substitution into marked variables with evidence, and its admissibility proof; (d) destructors on evidence once E4 adds destructors; (e) (INV-clock), the periodic clock rule from the r3 answer, encoded in Agda with the §9 example certified by it (spec §3.7; `marking-alternatives.md` §10.3). | `marking-alternatives.md` §6 | in progress | MALT |
| S23 | **Free objects (A1) as INIT instances.** Chapter 9 makes R-A and R-A⁺ instances of INIT (`universe-free-principles.md` §4.3). A free object ⟦Γ⟧ on a general context Γ should be the INIT instance whose context is Γ with its object variables read as points of X and its cell variables as cell variables of Δ (no X in any source). To check: that INIT's universal property (strong model morphisms, higher cells via cotensors) agrees with A1's LIFT schema at every level, including non-invertible cells. If it does, chapter 7 states A1 as that instance; chapter 7 keeps A1 as its own section either way, since chapters 7–8 come before INIT. | `spec/07-free-objects.md` §7.2–7.3; `spec/09-ordinary-math.md` §9.3 | **open** (2026-11-22) | SPEC thread |
| S24 | **`⊢hpol` is an optimisation but sits in the kernel.** `spec/04-context-checking.md` §4.4 places the judgment Γ ⊢hpol in a kernel chapter and §0.6 calls it "an optional optimisation" the kernel offers. It is never a premise of a kernel rule; its only effect is that a weight presentation may use Γ instead of a resolution (§4.3). Under Anthony's principle (spec §0.5, S19: optimisations live in layers above the kernel, each formalized and proved conservative), it belongs to that layer, with conservativity given by F(Γ) ≃ Free(Γ) for each base rule. Found in the spec walkthrough (Anthony, 2026-11-22: "if we only had the kernel, this concept wouldn't arise"). Fix: move §4.4 (and the §0.6 sentence) out of the kernel into the conservative-extension layer; restate its status as the conservativity obligation per base rule (currently Chanavat's homotopicality is cited, not formalised). | `spec/00-principles.md` §0.6; `spec/04-context-checking.md` §4.3–4.4; B25; S19 | **done** (2026-11-22): §4.4 retitled "Above the kernel: the fast path ⊢hpol", stated as a checker optimisation in the conservative-extension layer, with the conservativity obligation per rule (test accepts Γ ⟹ F(Γ) ≃ Free(Γ), so the trivial resolution is valid), formalized in Agda per rule; rules without a formalized obligation stay out of the fast path. Status: first base rule's decision procedure Agda-checked, its obligation open (Chanavat cited); the other rules open (B25). §0.6 and §0.3's rule-class list no longer name ⊢hpol as kernel; §4.3's "exactly when Γ ⊢hpol" corrected (⊢hpol is incomplete); README layer column updated | SPEC thread |
| S25 | **Closed-form normal data above degree four.** In V, V + S and T₁₂, each cell is a unique typed list of whole contextual records with neither native fork at a junction. A substring subdivision formula computes the list. Same-axis forks glue and the rank-four end-piece certificate settles the degree-five mixed-overlap case. | (K50), (K52), (K65); S3a | **Resolved** in [Researcher Response 1](conjectures/answered/directed-interchange-closed-form-above-four-2026-11-23.md#researcher-response-1), verified 2026-11-23 (scripts pass; the 2, 21, 272 rectangle counts recounted independently; the L-shaped pair of the attempt is untyped because it skips a corner; the d ≥ 6 overlap argument and the d = 5 strand reduction accepted at the argument level). The all-degree proof is on paper with a finite computational lemma. The Agda implementation belongs to S3a, as Route step 4 of `notes/uniform-checker.md` §1.8. | VSF |
| S26 | **§1.6 forbids the binders §0.2 allows.** `spec/01-core-syntax.md` §1.6 says later chapters "add no new definitional equalities and no new binders". Since S15, §0.2 explicitly allows Lawvere-style binding formers (ends, ∀X. T(X)) witnessed by cells, pending F10(c). Found in the spec walkthrough (piece 1.1, 2026-11-22). Fix: make §1.6 say that kernel extensions are constants, families of constants, `⊢ps`-style judgments, and (if F10(c) adopts any) binding formers in the sense of §0.2; and say what the syntax of such a binder is (it is not in §1.1's grammar). | `spec/01-core-syntax.md` §1.1, §1.6; `spec/00-principles.md` §0.2; F10(c) | **spec part done** (2026-11-23): §1.6 lists binding formers as a fourth kind of extension, with INIT as the kernel's one; §1.1 now has the binder `X. Δ` and INIT's term formers (μ, η_y, rec, rec_y, uni, uni_y), their scoping, and substitution acting on parameters (locally nameless bound variables, no shift, as in the Agda). Agda part S26a done by BIND; spec §1.1, §1.5 and §1.6 cite `CaTTExp.Binder.*` (2026-11-23). **done** | SPEC thread |
| S26b | **Agda: rec_y, uni and uni_y at cell variables.** `CaTTExp.Binder.Init` admits cell variables (any dimension) in μ, η_y and rec, but the arrow context Δ^→ (spec §9.3, model morphisms) has a component at each cell: at a k-cell, a (k+1)-cell between the two pastings of its naturality square. | `src/CaTTExp/Binder/Init.agda`, `InitLaws.agda`, `InitExamples.agda`; spec §9.3; F11 | **partly done** (2026-11-23): first, Δ^→ was stated at **globular 2-cells** l : f ⇒ g between map entries (`cell3Ty`: (η_l ▷ T(h)) · θ_g ⇛ θ_f · (S(h) ◁ σ_l)), so rec_y at such cells, uni on contexts of maps and globular 2-cells, and uni_y at map entries of such contexts are kernel terms, with their laws; the circle (X, b, l : b ⇒ b) checks with all of them (`InitExamples`, the rec_y type at l pinned). **(a) done** (2026-11-23): uni_y at a globular 2-cell l : f ⇒ g is the 4-cell `mod4` between two pastings of 3-cells, (interchange, θ′_l, uni_f) and (uni_g, θ_l, interchange), with associators; selection, substitution law and scoping re-proved (`InitLaws.uniY2Body-[]`, `sc-uniY2Body`), and on the circle uni_y at l is checked with a well-formed type (`InitExamples`). **Remaining:** **(b)** 2-cells with composite boundaries (the torus, p ; q ⇒ q ; p; the companion cells of S26c step 2), which need θ of a composite: unblocked by S26h step 2 (PR #524, `Naturality.DimOne`, every term of dimension ≤ 1), BIND ports the recursion to binder syntax: **step 1 done** (2026-11-23), `Binder.Cyl` (the construction of `Naturality.Raw` over binder syntax, the selected variables being one binder's bound variables), `Binder.CylLaws` (substitution by a locally closed σ and scoping, for any scoped filler family), `Binder.CylOne` (K₁ scoped after embedding), and `Binder.CylExamples` (on the torus, l↑'s type, p↑'s type and (p ; q)↑ equal the embedded pure ones, by refl); **step 2 done** (2026-11-23): INIT rebased on the cylinders (`Binder.Init`, `InitLaws`, `Binder.CylOK`). The component of Δ^→ at entry y is `cylTy` of bvar 0 y (datum: m⁻ = η, m⁺ = the model, every entry selected), so rec_y at y is the cylinder of y, and uni_y at y is a second-level cylinder, of θ_y over the arrow context. The side condition `goodTy` admits an entry when every touched coherence in its type is over a pure pasting context with a pure type of dimension 1 (the coherences K₁ fills), so cells of any dimension with such boundaries and composite boundaries are covered; identity factors at X-free sources and targets are now omitted, as in `Naturality`. Laws re-proved from `CylLaws` (substitution and scoping; there is no typing theorem for binder cylinders yet, so well-formedness of these result types is checked per example and in general is INIT's `res-wf`, S26i), much faster than the hand-written `cell3Ty` and `mod4`, which are removed. Checked (`InitExamples`): ℕ (rec_y shapes pinned), even/odd, the circle, a 3-cell entry m : l ⇛ l (rec_y a 4-cell, uni), rec_y at the companion and uni over a based telescope (S26c), and a fixed point e : z ; s ⇒ z; result types `wfTy`. **Regression:** uni_y at a 2-cell (part (a)'s `mod4`) is rejected until C1d (2), since θ_l's boundaries are 2-dimensional coherences (owner NATF via C1d; BIND re-admits it once the filler lands). **Correction:** in INIT points are maps ⊤ → X, so the torus's loops p, q are 2-cells and p ; q is a 2-dimensional coherence: the torus needs C1d (2), not `DimOne` (NATF told). **(c)** cells of dimension ≥ 3: covered when their boundaries are variables or dimension-1 coherences (step 2); the rest needs S26h at dimension ≥ 2. | BIND |
| S26c | **Agda: several sorts over bases (F11b) in the kernel.** Spec §9.3 (PR #503) allows finitely many sort variables X_i, each optionally over a base B_i that replaces ⊤ as terminal target. | `src/CaTTExp/Binder/Init.agda`; spec §9.3; F11b | **step 1 done** (2026-11-23): several sorts over ⊤. The telescope opens with its sorts X_0, …, X_{n-1} : ⋆; μ_k, rec_k and uni_k are indexed by sorts, η_y, rec_y and uni_y by later entries; sources and targets may be any sort. Laws re-proved; checked example: even/odd naturals (E, O, z : ⊤ → E, s : E → O, t : O → E) with all six formers (`InitExamples`). **Step 2 (bases)**, in the syntax HIT fixed: each based sort has a structure map p_i : X_i → B_i in the header; an operation a : S → X_i over B_i carries s : S → B_i and an **invertible** cell a ; p_i ≅ s; model morphisms carry invertible cells h_i ; p′_i ≅ p_i. **Step 2 done** (2026-11-23), without waiting on S26e: the `inv` on a companion is a mark in Δ (S26d), so a model must certify it through the kernel's mark premise. `positive` now also checks `basedOK`: structure maps p_k : X_k → B_k (B_k X-free, at most one per sort, increasing k) form a header right after the sorts; in the body an X-free target must be ⊤ unless the entry is a companion; an operation a : S → X_k into a based sort must be a map immediately followed by its companion c : a ; p_k ⇒ s marked inv, with s a term (HIT's correction: s is not a new variable). Stability under substitution re-proved (`InitLaws.basedOK-stable`). Checked (`InitExamples`): in context (B, s : ⊤ → B), μ(X, p : X → B, a : ⊤ → X, c : a ; p ⇒ s inv) with μ, η at every entry, rec (and its rejection when the model's companion is unmarked), rec_y at p and at a; rejections of a missing, unmarked or ill-shaped companion, a cell into a based sort, an uncompanioned X-free target, and a header after the body. **Remaining:** rec_y at the companion and uni over a based telescope are **done** (2026-11-23, S26b (b) step 2: θ of a; p is the K₁ cylinder); cells of dimension ≥ 2 into a based sort (the syntax for their companions is not fixed; ask HIT). Known completeness loss (audit nit on #525): the header is read greedily, so a map X_k → B right after the sorts is always a structure map; μ(X, t : X → ⊤, z : ⊤ → X) was accepted before step 2 and is now rejected (z lacks a companion). Sound; lifting it needs the header marked explicitly (BIND, with S26d's marks). | BIND |
| S26d | **Agda: marks in INIT contexts.** Spec §9.3/§9.5 (PR #511): an `inv` mark in Δ is chapter 3's declaration mark `(y : A)^inv`; models must derive `y[σ] inv` (marks are preserved by substitution). `⋆^{≤n}` and `grpd` restrict the sorts. The binder kernel has no marks, so ℤ = μ(X, z, s inv) and S¹ via `X : grpd` are not yet kernel terms. To do: the mark judgments of `CaTTExp.Gray.Marks` in the binder kernel (shared with S26e), then marked telescopes and their positivity clauses. | `src/CaTTExp/Binder/Init.agda`; spec §3.2–3.3, §9.3, §9.5 | **inv marks done** (2026-11-23). Telescopes can carry marks (S26e step 1). INIT's `positive` now accepts `inv` on operations and cells, keeps the sorts unmarked, and rejects every other mark. A model is an argument list for Δ itself, so the kernel's mark premise makes it certify each inv mark. Checked on ℤ = μ(X, z, s : X → X inv) (`InitExamples`): rec into (A, z′, s′) is accepted with s′ marked inv and rejected with s′ unmarked, and rec_y, uni and uni_y check. "η_s inv" in the generic model is the mark judgment's: MARK's `formerInv` hook, asked to take the telescope. **Sort marks done** (2026-11-23). `Binder.Mark` has a truncation mark `mtrunc k` for ⋆^{≤k−2}, and positivity accepts `grpd_k` and ⋆^{≤n} on sorts. Models certify these marks on their carriers through the kernel's mark premise. Checked (`InitExamples`): S¹ = μ(X : grpd₁, b, l : b ⇒ b) with rec and rec_y at l, and ‖A‖₀ = μ(X : ⋆^{≤0}, g : A → X). rec is accepted with the carrier carrying the mark and rejected otherwise. **Remaining**, all in the mark judgment (MARK's Gray port, S26e): μ_k carries its sort's marks, η_y carries inv when entry y does, and the trunc rules of §9.1 (T-init, T-fill) on binder syntax. Follow-up for S26e: under the (MARK)-only judgment marks match exactly, so a carrier with a smaller truncation (mtrunc 1 for ‖A‖₀) or another grpd_j is rejected; once the Gray judgment adds cumulativity, the smaller-truncation case should become an acceptance (re-pin in `InitExamples`), while mtrunc 3 stays rejected (audit nit on #521). | BIND |
| S26e | **Agda: invertibility of INIT's cells.** INIT-J1/J2 give **invertible** cells. Spec §9.3 (PR #511): this is chapter 3's judgment `Γ ⊢ t inv`, with the destructors DES (t⁻ˡ, t⁻ʳ, λ_t, ρ_t) and DES-inv as bi-invertibility data at every level, so no new formers: the kernel needs rules concluding `rec_y(…) inv`, `uni(…) inv`, `uni_y(…) inv`. `CaTTExp.Gray.Marks` has the inv rules but **not** DES (its header: "Not encoded: … DES"); DES in Agda is ledger E4 (MARK). The three INIT rules are syntax-directed axioms on the head former, so `inv` stays decidable (spec §9.3 after PR #513; §3.7's undecidability needs inv-intro, which `inv` lacks). To do once E4 lands: the inv judgment with DES in the binder kernel, then the three rules. | `src/CaTTExp/Binder/`; `src/CaTTExp/Gray/Marks.agda`; spec §3.2–3.3, §9.3; E4 | **step 1 done** (2026-11-23): marked contexts in the binder kernel, in the shape agreed with MARK. An entry is `Γ ▸[ ms ] A`, with marks inv, ps and grpd_k (`Binder.Mark`). The kernel takes a mark judgment `mark : Ctx → Mark → Tm → Bool` as a parameter. Substitutions and binder arguments must satisfy the marks of their entries, and pasting contexts are unmarked. Substitution and weakening are re-proved under three stability hypotheses: MarkRen, MarkSub (both conditional on typing) and MarkVar. The checker and its completeness are re-proved. Conservativity holds for every mark judgment. `Binder.VarMark` proves the three hypotheses for the judgment with only rule (MARK), and `MarkExamples` checks it. MARK's `Gray/DesSide` is updated so that erasure drops marks. **Step 2 done** (2026-11-23): the DES formers form a binder instance (`Binder.Des`, `DesLaws`, `DesExamples`). There is one former for each kind (linv, rinv, unitL, unitR) and each dimension n ≥ 1. Each has an empty telescope and selector 0. Its arguments are Dⁿ with the cell marked inv, so (DES)'s premise `t inv` is the kernel's mark premise. The result types are those of §3.3, with t⁻ˡ ; t, t ; t⁻ʳ and id as disk coherences. The construction is generic in the former set via an injection, so DES can sit beside INIT. `desLaws` proves BinderLaws over any binder-free 𝕊₀. Conservativity follows from `Embed` (for L, from MARK's `Gray/DesSide`, with `cellArgD`). Checked: at 1- and 2-cells all four types are pinned or shown well formed by the checker, and an unmarked cell is rejected. (DES-inv) belongs to the mark judgment, via `formerInv` in MARK's port. MARK's `Gray/MarksB` landed (DES and DES-inv as the `mark` parameter, MarkRen/MarkSub proved). **Step 3 done** (2026-11-23): INIT and DES in one signature (`Binder.InitDes`). INIT's construction and laws and DES's laws are now generic in the former set (`Init.Construction K B inj`, `InitLawsGen`, `DesLawsGen`), and the combined laws are theirs former by former. INIT's inv rules are its `formerMarks` in `Gray/MarksB`: rec_y, uni and uni_y are inv; μ_k and η_y carry the marks of their telescope entry (the generic model satisfies the telescope's marks); rec carries none. These are syntax-directed axioms, so `inv` stays decidable. FormerStable and CellDim are proved, so `Gray/MarksBKernel` gives MarkSub/MarkRen and substitution for L + INIT + DES, under the same parameters as DES alone (closed constants; presupposition `tm-ty`, S26i). Checked (`InitDesExamples`, with plain CaTT's coh side condition): on ℕ, rec_z and rec_s are inv and rec is not; all four destructors of rec_z and rec_s are accepted, and rec⁻ˡ is rejected; uni and uni_s are inv, with left inverses accepted; η_s is inv when s is marked inv; μ is grpd₁ when X is. **Limit:** L's side condition on binder syntax (`graySideD`) erases INIT's formers to nothing, so under it no coherence mentioning an INIT term is derivable (INIT's own result types use such coherences). Lifting this needs an erasure of INIT terms to L, e.g. by treating μ, rec, … as opaque constants of the right type (owner MARK, agreed 2026-11-23: which support μ, rec, η terms get in K(Γ), checking DimOK and CellDim; starts from `InitDes`). Adjacent: S26b part 2, S26g | BIND |
| S26f | **Agda: the full positivity check.** `positive` checks the fragment whose sources are X or X-free and whose targets are X or ⊤. Which constants are ⊤ is declared by the base signature (`Base.top?`) and not checked: INIT's soundness for targets T = ⊤ assumes the declared constants are terminal. Checking it needs a terminality judgment or ⊤ as a kernel former (chapter 2). Spec §9.3 also allows targets Hom_X(x, y), (lax) cotensors [A, X], and finite limits of these, and sources built with limits, colimits, ⊗ and ×. Those are not term formers of the binder kernel yet (they are the limit and Gray rules of chapters 2–7); each clause can be added to `entryOK` with its stability law once its former is in the binder kernel. | `src/CaTTExp/Binder/Init.agda`; spec §9.3 | blocked (2026-11-23) on **S26g**; adjacent work S26b–S26e | BIND |
| S26g | **Agda: limit, colimit and Gray formers in the binder kernel.** The full positivity check (S26f) needs Hom_X(x, y), (lax) cotensors [A, X], finite limits, colimits, ⊗ and × as term formers of `CaTTExp.Binder` so that `entryOK` can inspect them. Today they live in binder-free kernels (`CaTTExp.Lim.*`, `CaTTExp.Gray.*`, …) over `CaTTExp.Syntax`. To do: give each as a constant family or binder-interface instance of `CaTTExp.Binder.Signature`, with its laws, and prove it conservative over its binder-free kernel via `Embed`. | `src/CaTTExp/Binder/`; spec chapters 2–7, §9.3 | in progress (2026-11-23), after S26b–S26e. Agreed 2026-11-23 with the S27 thread ("Extend Agda metatheory to operation heads"): limit heads (lim, λ, uni of chapter 6) come from its generic head interface `CaTTExp.Binder.Heads` (S27 (3), owner that thread), which S26g/S26f import; colimits, ⊗ and × stay here. **Colimits, ⊗ and ×** moved 2026-11-23 from BIND to the thread "Add colimit, tensor and product formers to binder kernel" (COLIM): ⊗, \ and / as GB-form heads first; × is derived as lim over two points (spec §6.5) from S27's lim head, so no separate former; colim, cocone legs and couni as a second instance of S27's head interface with the cocone shapes. Limit heads (lim, λ, uni, Hom_X, cotensors): S27. **Step 1 done** (2026-11-23, COLIM): ⊗, \ and / of (GB-form) are heads (`Binder.GrayForm`): arity (A B : ⋆), type ⋆, as an empty-telescope binder family generic in the former set via an injection. Laws former by former (`GrayFormLawsGen`), BinderLaws and PresupLaws over any 𝕊₀ (`GrayFormLaws`), (GB-form) derivable for every side condition and mark judgment with an inversion lemma (`gb-wf`, `gb-inv`), and the biclosed object formulas of `Gray.Biclosed.Syntax` sent to well-typed kernel objects (`GrayFormEmbed.fm-wf`; ⟦Θ⟧ and ⟦Θ⟧♭ have no image until chapter 7's heads exist). Conservativity is `Embed.CaTT 𝕊₀ (grayFormBinders K)`. Checked (`GrayFormExamples`): nested formulas typed, a map out of A ⊗ B and a 2-cell between such maps, five rejections. **Not covered:** ⊗'s universal property, which lives in the multimap judgment (spec §8.1–8.3), not a kernel judgment; it is formalized over its own syntax in `Gray/Biclosed`. Its entry into the kernel is spec §8.1's open item "implementing the multimap judgment in the main checker". **Step 2 done** (2026-11-23, COLIM): colimits are the second instance of S27's `LimConstruction` (spec §6.3), over the specified cocone of `Cones.CoconeOf` (`Binder.Colim`): colim_Γ (arity Γ, type ⋆) and the cocone legs q_{Γ,g}, in one HeadSpec family `limColimSpec` with the limit heads (former set LCF, a copy of LimF for each), so `HeadSpec.Laws` gives BinderLaws and the (op) rule (`LimColimLaws`). Checked (`ColimExamples`): the colimit of an arrow F : A → B with q_x : A → C, q_y : B → C and q_f : q_x ⇒ F ; q_y (the cocone cell reverses the leg); the coproduct as the colimit over two points with its coprojections; the colimit over the empty base; limits in the same signature; three rejections; conservativity via `Embed.CaTT`. × is S27's lim over two points (`LimHeadsExamples`, π₁, π₂), so it needs no former of its own. **Remaining:** couni over the cocone-transfor shape (like uni, it needs horn arities; S27 HEADS); bases that are not globular (the specified cocone, like the cone, is defined on globular bases); the presupposition law for HeadSpec families (not proved for lim either). Like lim, colim and its legs do not suspend in general (S27 (g)). | COLIM (colimits, ⊗, ×); S27 (limit heads) |
| S26h | **Agda: θ of a composite (the naturality filler N, mechanized).** Given a cylinder datum on Γ (two substitutions m⁻, m⁺, an up-closed selection Y, and θ_x : a_x → b_x for x ∈ Y; unselected boundaries omitted), define cyl t by recursion on t (cyl (var x) = θ_x, cyl (coh Q B γ) = a filler K over Q), prove Δ ⊢ cyl t : a_t → b_t and that cyl commutes with substitution on both sides, parametric in a typed filler family K; then instances of K through dimension 1 (identities, chain composites), then dimension ≥ 2 (notes/relative-coherence.md §4). Unblocks S26b (b), (c) and the rest of S26c. | notes/relative-coherence.md §4, notes/naturality-arbitrary-depth.md §2.2; C1 | in progress (2026-11-23); same item as C1d, status tracked there (recursion, both substitution laws, typing, cylinder contexts and all dimension-1 fillers done in PR #524; dimension ≥ 2 open) | NATF |
| S26i | **Agda: presupposition in the binder kernel.** `tm-ty : Γ ⊢tm t ∶ A → Γ ⊢ty A` for `TypingWith 𝕊 side mark` over a SigOK signature. The `bnd` case needs a new `BinderLaws` field: under its side condition, `bres b y Δ` is well formed over Γ ⧺ openTel Δ, so `opTy 0 ts (bres …)` is well formed once ts ⊢args. To do: the field, its DES and INIT instances, and tm-ty. Needed by MARK's MarkSub for Gray marks (fuel stability and DimOK on the mark-free instance), which takes it as a module parameter until then. | `src/CaTTExp/Binder/`; Gray/MarksSubst, MarksFuel, CohDim | **in progress** (2026-11-23): generic part and DES done. `CaTTExp.Binder.Presup`: `PresupLaws` (`bargs-sc`, `res-wf`), `SigOK`, and `tm-ty` for any instance satisfying Subst's hypotheses (closed, BinderLaws, MarkRen/Sub/Var), so in particular for a mark-free instance; also `fresh-args` and `args-sub′` (well-typed arguments are a substitution from the opened telescope). `CaTTExp.Binder.DesPresup`: DES's `res-wf` for every n (opened disk telescope, the destructors typed by the binder rule with the inv premise by MarkVar, unit-law composites and identities typed by the coh rule with their pasting contexts proved pasting for every n through the pure judgment) and `des-tm-ty`, under two side-condition hypotheses: the coh side accepts the composite over Dⁿ⁺¹ ∨ Dⁿ⁺¹ (`side-comp`) and the identity over Dⁿ (`side-id`). For plain CaTT both hold for every n (`CaTTExp.Binder.DesSideCatt`: the composite is `Derived`'s head BK n 0, accepted by `side-BK0`; the identity over Dⁿ is a coherence; transferred to binder syntax by `CaTTExp.Binder.PureSide.cattSide-pure`), so `catt-tm-ty` is presupposition for plain CaTT + DES with the (MARK)-only judgment, given only Closed and SigOK (𝕊₀'s constants have well-formed types). (1) side-comp/side-id for L's side (`graySideD`): **done** by MARK (PR #533, `Gray/DesPresupL`: `free-tm-ty`, `gray-tm-ty`, and `gray-tm[]` for L + DES with Gray marks, given Closed and SigOK). **Remaining:** (2) INIT's `res-wf` [plan, BIND 2026-11-23: port NAT's Typing/Boundary/Commute to binder syntax over free variables (`Binder.VCyl`, `VCylLaws` (substitution laws, done), `PureEmbed` (pure CaTT derivations embed, done)); because res-wf cannot call the kernel's tm-ty (it is what tm-ty needs), the ported typing carries *deep* type derivations (every boundary of a type typed), and PresupLaws.res-wf gets a deep form of its context premise, computed in Presup's bnd case from the premise's subderivations. **Done so far** (2026-11-23): `Binder.Deep` (deep types `DTy`, deep contexts, substitution and weakening, coherences over embedded pure contexts with their deep types), `Binder.VWhisk` (spheres, disks, both whiskers' classifying substitutions and the whiskers themselves typed with deep types, and their types `rwT-ty0`, `rwT-ty`, `lwT-ty0`, `lwT-ty`, via an agreement lemma between sphere positions and `baseAt`/`srcAt`/`tgtAt`), `Binder.VBoundary` (Boundary's theorem in binder syntax: `P-wf`, `L-wf`, `cylTy-wf`, a_t → b_t a deep type given deep cylinders of the touched boundaries; the fact that untouched terms have untouched types is a hypothesis `but`, proved for suffix selections, the form INIT uses, from scoping alone (`Suffix.but-suffix`); for general selections it needs the free variables of binder result types). Next: Typing's `cyl-wf` (γ↑ typed, the generic datum and cylinder context in binder syntax), fillers through the embedding (binder `fillK` only fills coherences whose type has variable boundaries, which covers `CylOK`'s dimension-1 coherences), the opening bridge to `Binder.Cyl`, then res-wf for the six formers] (rec_y and uni_y types are coherence composites over model-morphism data; uni's argument telescope needs `morTel` well formed), BIND, needed once INIT joins the Gray signature (S26e step 3). Caveat found while scoping it: the η_y result type is A_y with the generic model substituted, and A_y may apply a former whose arguments carry marks (e.g. a destructor at an inv-marked entry s, which becomes η_s). So INIT's `res-wf` needs the mark judgment to certify the generic model's marks (μ_k and η_j carry the marks of Δ, the S26d/S26e rule). That holds for the mark-free instance and is meant to hold for the Gray judgment, but fails for the (MARK)-only `varMark` on such telescopes (a non-variable has no marks there), so a combined INIT + DES instance with `varMark` would not satisfy presupposition. The Gray judgment of S26e step 3 (`Binder.InitDes.initDesMarks`) gives μ_k and η_j exactly the marks of Δ's entries. | BIND |
| S26j | **Agda: the multimap judgment in the binder kernel.** ⊗, \ and / are kernel heads (S26g step 1, `Binder.GrayForm`), but their universal properties (corollas m, e^ℓ, e^r, p, pt; the cut templates GB-cut and GB-dcut) are stated over the multimap judgment (X₁, …, Xₙ ▸ Z) of spec §8.1–8.3, which the binder kernel does not have; today it lives over its own syntax in `Gray/Biclosed` (formulas, templates, `admit?`). Spec §8.1 lists "implementing the multimap judgment in the main checker" as open. Formulas are read with the GrayForm heads. Stability laws (`MultimapLaws.gmLaws`) and presupposition (`MultimapPresup.gmPresup`: the opened arity is a well-formed context and the type is well formed, for every side condition and mark judgment) for the Gray formers and mm together, over any 𝕊₀; conservativity is `Embed.CaTT`. Checked (`MultimapExamples`): from f : A ⊗ B → C its curries B → A\C and A → C/B (c of the evaluation rows at n = 0); from g : B → A\C its uncurry A ⊗ B → C (c of the pair row applied to e^ℓ ∘ (1, g)); composites and identities; six rejections. **Step 2 done** (2026-11-23): cells between unary multimaps are kernel cells (`Binder.MultimapCells`). A kernel k-cell between maps X → Z is a k-cell of 𝒦(X, Z) = Hom_lax(X, Z) = X\Z (`conventions.md` §4), so by the left-evaluation row a multimap (X, ⟦Dᵏ⟧ ▸ Z): admitted cells are `hom m 1 …` (time slot in gap 1) over unary operations; inv entries are kernel entries marked inv. Head family mmc_{k,Φ,t}, whose type's boundaries are the kernel images of biclosed terms: a variable is its argument, any other term is the mmc head of its **canonical support** (used entries closed under dependencies, in order of first use; used atoms in order of first occurrence; renumbered), and every head must be canonical (side condition, like coh's support condition), so each biclosed term has one kernel image. Stability laws (`MultimapCellsLaws.gcLaws`, now from `HeadSpec`). Checked (`MultimapCellsExamples`): the curry head applied to f and to g; from a kernel 2-cell β : f ⇒ g between maps A ⊗ B → C, (GB-dcut) on the left-evaluation row gives a kernel 2-cell curry(f) ⇒ curry(g) whose boundaries are exactly the curry head on f and on g; rejections (unused entry, unused atom, gap 0, wrong boundary). **Presupposition done** (2026-11-23): mmc and the Gray formers are restated as one `HeadSpec` family (`MultimapCells.cellSpec`; laws, honest (op) rule and checker from `HeadSpec.Laws`), and every valid head has a well-formed type over its arity in the kernel with plain CaTT's side condition and rule (MARK) (`MultimapCellsPresup.ok-all`, `cells-presup`, `cells-tm-ty`), as `LimPresup` does for lim (with an arbitrary mark judgment, inv-marked arguments of boundary heads cannot be typed). The proof: a boundary that is not a variable is typed by rule (op) at its canonical head, whose side condition holds (`MultimapCellsWf.sub-mmcTy`; its arity and type are scoped and locally closed syntactically, `MultimapCellsScope`), whose arguments are variables of the prefix, and whose arity and type, opened at the arguments, are the images over Φ (`MultimapCellsComm`: the kernel images commute with the renaming onto a support, since canonical heads compose with it, `MultimapCellsCompose.canon-comp`). No step recurses into the canonical heads' own presupposition. Two changes to the head on the way: a support now starts from the entries of the term **and of its type** (`supStart`; a type can use entries its term does not), and mmcTy also asks that the inferred type be well formed and use only atoms below k (needed for the type's boundaries to have canonical heads). The examples are unchanged. Invertible results are not passed to the kernel's mark judgment (an mmc result of mode inv is a plain kernel cell; MARK's `formerMarks` could mark it). **Nullary multimaps done** (2026-11-23): a point ( ▸ C) is a kernel map ⊤ → C, with ⊤ the point object: a `unit` head joins the cell family, with exactly the rule of `LimHeads`' lim_ε (`MultimapCells.unit-is-lim`). A k-cell between points has its time slot in gap 0 (the only gap), so the admitted gap is the number of inputs of the base (`inputs`: 1 for X ▸ Z, 0 for ▸ Z). Presupposition and laws extend (`MultimapCellsPresup`, `MultimapCellsLaws`). Checked (`MultimapCellsExamples`): from x, y : ⊤ → A, α : x ⇒ y and f : A → B, f ∘ x : ⊤ → B and the whiskered 2-cell f ∘ α : f ∘ x ⇒ f ∘ y, whose boundaries are the canonical head on x and on y; a cell between points at gap 1 is rejected. ⊤'s universal property (uni for the empty cone) is not encoded, as for lim in general (S27). **Remaining:** n ≥ 2 multimaps are reachable only inside biclosed terms (as kernel 1-cells out of tensors, through the pair row's c), and binary-multimap cells such as the n = 0 comparison ε are not kernel cells. **Sort discipline done** (2026-11-23): `infer` admits AdmI instances, a strict superset of the data-sorted kernel instances `KInst` (`Gray.Biclosed.SortDisc`), so the side conditions of mm and mmc now also ask that the context, the term and (for mmc) the type be sorted (`Multimap.sortedT`, `sortedY`): every here node sends its template's data variable to a non-representable formula whose head is an atom, which implies SortDisc's `DSub` into the atoms (`MultimapSorted.dataInst-dsub`; the converse is not claimed). Canonical heads stay sorted (`MultimapSorted.Ren`), so mm's and mmc's presupposition laws are kept; the examples are unchanged, and an instance that infers but sends the data variable to ⟦D⁰⟧ is rejected (`MultimapCellsExamples.unsorted`). Still inherited from `infer`: `glue`'s typing rule is stated in the biclosed checker, not derived from L. mmc is a HeadSpec instance; mm (step 1) can be restated the same way (its laws would then also give the honest (op) rule). | COLIM |

| S28 | **User postulates.** `spec/01` §1.6 said a development's "postulated objects, cells and marks are a signature (equivalently, a context it is checked in)". The sentence dates from the first spec draft (8da1b2e) with no note behind it. Anthony (walkthrough, 2026-11-23): users must not be able to assert that something exists; postulates are incompatible with the design. The "equivalently" was also false, since constants cannot be substituted or suspended and context variables can. **Fixed in spec:** the signature is closed to users, every head is a kernel former with rules and a model interpretation, and user assumptions are context variables (hypothetical, asserting nothing). Remaining: any surface design (Q1.x, ch. 3 marks) must offer hypotheses only as context variables, including postulated marks. | SPEC | fixed (spec); surface constraint recorded |

---

## Out to external researchers

**Paste-ready prompts live in `conjectures/outbox/`.** Each is standalone and contains only the prompt (no status metadata; Anthony appends responses). Sent/unsent status is tracked here and in `conjectures/README.md`. Every follow-up round gets a new filename (`<name>-r2.md`, `<name>-r3.md`, …), so a file that has received a response is never reused. The outbox holds only unsent or awaiting-response prompts; answered prompts move, with response and verification, to `conjectures/answered/`.

**Sent, awaiting response:** none.

**Unsent (ready to send, paste as is).** None from MALT (`invertibility-certificates` r1, r2 and r3 answered and verified 2026-11-23; in `answered/`). None from MKL. (`invertibility-in-directed-interchange.md` was answered 2026-11-21 and moved to `answered/`.) (`sd-cocartesian.md`, written 2026-11-21, was retired unsent the same day: closed in-house.)

Before 2026-11-21 there were none: the last open prompt, relative-coherence r10, was answered. On 2026-11-18 the incremental per-round lines were consolidated into general prompts that each ask for a whole line at once (thread "Consolidate researcher prompts into ambitious asks"). The never-sent incremental prompts they replace are in `conjectures/retired/` (see its README). **Rule since 2026-11-20:** a follow-up stays here only if its answer would change a rule, the kernel or checker design, or a construction the project needs; otherwise it goes to `conjectures/retired/` with a one-line reason.

**Answered 2026-11-18 and verified** (in `conjectures/answered/`): naturality-arbitrary-depth (rounds 1–8), directed-interchange-every-level (rounds 1–8), gray-contexts (rounds 1–8), cone-over-coherences (rounds 1–5), gray-closed-scheduling (rounds 1–7), gray-tensor-of-contexts-rule, catt-models-in-infinity-omega (rounds 1–6), catt-models-unit-condition (2026-11-19), relative-coherence-all-dimensions, homotopy-polygraphs, gray-biclosed-coherence, canonical-gray-terms, lax-interchange-every-dimension-r2, fibration-presentations-characterization-r2, catt-models-unit-condition-r2 (all 2026-11-19), fibration-presentations-all-dimensions (2026-11-19), lax-interchange-construction-or-impossibility (2026-11-19), and the round of 2026-11-19 13:39 UTC: relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r2, homotopy-polygraphs-r2, gray-biclosed-coherence-r2, canonical-gray-terms-r2, lax-interchange-every-dimension-r3 (as-sent text restored), fibration-presentations-characterization-r3, catt-models-unit-condition-r3 (all verified 2026-11-19), steiner-nu-freeness, steiner-support-formalizable, strict-omega-pasting-formal, dir-relative-coherence (rounds 1 and 2), non-derivability-q2, finite-fibration-test, finite-fibration-test-2d-base, finite-fibration-test-higher-base (rounds 1–6), lax-functor-refinement, gray-pasting-types, cartesian-closed-higher-coherence, non-derivability.

**Responses received, awaiting verification:** none.

## Closed

Kept briefly for orientation; details are in the linked files.

- S26a (Agda: binders in the kernel syntax, so §1.3–1.5 reach INIT's terms): **closed 2026-11-23**, thread "Extend the Agda kernel with binders", PR #506 (honesty review PASS, https://github.com/AHartNtkn/CaTTExp/pull/506#issuecomment-7104557769). `src/CaTTExp/Binder/`: a general locally nameless binder interface with substitution admissibility (`Subst`), certified checker, completeness and decidability (`CheckWith`, `Completeness`), conservativity over the binder-free kernel (`Embed`, unconditional for CaTT), and INIT as an instance (`Init`, `InitLaws`, `InitMeta`, `InitEmbed`) whose J1/J2 shapes are pinned on ℕ in `InitExamples`. Remaining extensions are S26b–S26f.
- S17 (the form of the marking system was never compared against alternatives; spec walkthrough 2026-11-21): **compared**, thread "Compare alternatives to the marker system", `marking-alternatives.md`. Forced: marks not a second sort (Thm 3.5.1), `ps*` (C1″), `ord` through the side condition (E13); not forced: judgments vs evidence. Findings: `inv` is not saturated (Agda, `Gray/MarksSaturation.agda`); no sound saturated judgment on raw terms is decidable (Thm 3.3, proved, via the unit problem of f.p. monoids); (DES-inv) was missing (spec §3.3 repaired, Agda with E4). Verdict: keep judgments, add an equation-free evidence layer (S21), read `ord`/`grpd_k` as modes, reject marked arrow types and the complicial form. Follow-ups S21, S22.
- S22 (are finite certificates complete for invertibility in free L-models?): **closed 2026-11-23, negatively, twice.** r2: a finite marked computad has an invertible 3-cell outside every finite certificate. r3: invertibility in free L-models is not recursively enumerable (nonhalting reduces to it, even for one fixed computad), so no decidably checked certificate language is complete and none is maximal. `inv` in L is permanently incomplete; in P certificates are complete. Follow-up (INV-clock) moved to S21 (e). The weak case's citations (Ozornova–Rovelli–Walde §5; Gepner–Heine 3.8.1, 3.8.3, 4.5.4, 4.8.17, 4.9.1) were checked against the sources the same day (MALT), so the weak result does not rest on unchecked citations. Verified answers `conjectures/answered/invertibility-certificates-r2-2026-11-23.md`, `-r3-2026-11-23.md`; `marking-alternatives.md` §§9–10.

- D7h (the coherence term `here A` inhabited every well-formed type, forgetting the template instance; found 2026-11-20 while formalizing Theorem S): **repaired**, thread "Formalize the strict Gray model in Agda". `HereAmbiguity.agda` is the machine-checked witness. The term is now `here t e φs ts`, typed by CaTT's rule for coh(Γ, A)[σ]: `Typing.infer` checks the template side conditions and that each argument has the substituted type of its template entry (`argsOk`). `sub-infer` covers the new term, `templates-typed` now assumes okT, and the new `Scope` module proves every okT template well scoped and the identity instance of c typed.

- D18 (Agda proof of termination and confluence of N/β/η for 1-cells of 𝒢, reading I): **proved**, `src/CaTTExp/Gray/NBE/` (`--safe`, no postulates), summarized in `closed-structure.md` F.7. `Termination.sn` uses the lexicographic measure (n, Σ_nodes (n − depth)). `Confluence` proves local confluence over all critical pairs (the seven rows in both orientations, plus the same-position, variable and disjoint cases) and confluence via Newman's lemma. `Decide` gives normal forms, Church–Rosser and `decide : Dec (u ≈ v)` from decidable equality of factors and labels; `Examples` runs it. `Typing` proves subject reduction (`sr`), so the untyped results restrict to typed words (`Typing.typed-confluence`, and `Decide.TypedDecide`: `typed-church-rosser`, `normalize-typed`, `typed-decide`). The untyped N has no guard; the checker's `nonempty Xn` is automatic on typed words. The #238 review notes were fixed in #239.
- B9 (collapse in all dimensions ≥ 3): **proved**, `limits-metatheory.md` §3. A d-cell is the top f-component of a (d−2)-transfor over 1_Y, and `uni` at level d−1 inverts it. Only the full-support coherence rule is needed, not BM Prop. 35. Machine check: B16.
- B10 (models of the intended CaTT_lim rules): **classified**, `limits-metatheory.md` §4. They are exactly the (∞,ω)-categories with cells of dimension ≥ 3 invertible that have finite GHL/Loubaton lax limits (e.g. Cat), conditional on B5 and E11.
- B11 (horn judgment): **done as a design**, `limits-metatheory.md` §5.3. Agda encoding: B15.
- B12 (one schema): **done as a design**, `limits-metatheory.md` §8. The alternating variance is a condition on the index (B14), not on the cone schema. Under L, colimit transfors take the diagram index first. Full duality is not a symmetry of L: it reverses the interchanger (passed to D8).
- B13 (Hom_⊖): **identified at the generator level under P**. Loubaton's ⊖ is Gray in the first transfor direction and cartesian above it (Construction 3.1.2.15, Prop. 3.1.2.17), which is the repaired table. Under L the cone categories are the full Gray homs (Theorem A, strict). Full identification of the shapes: B5. See `limits-metatheory.md` §7.
- `free-on-graph.md`: solved (researcher, verified). N ≃ ℕ_disc semantically.
- `weighted-as-partially-lax-omega.md`: solved in Loubaton's model (researcher, verified). Leaves B12–B14.
- `gray-vs-cartesian.md`: C1, C1′ proved; C1″ without "locally full" (researcher, verified). Leaves E4's recursive marking.
- `catt-lim-pseudo-vs-gray.md`: answered (researcher, verified). Leaves B1–B4, B9, B10.
- `lax-colimit-of-point.md`: semantic claim solved; arbitrary-derivation syntactic form refuted. Leaves B5, B6.
- `free-infinity-gray-of-pasting-diagrams.md`: solved in the literature. Discharges (D)–(F) of `pseudo-vs-gray.md` §3.6.
- `planar-pasting-types.md` PL1 (D9): proved directly, `closed-structure.md` Part A. Units (D10): contractible criterion, Part B.
- `steiner-support-determines-cells.md`: solved for the needed case (Hadzihasanovic). Discharges (A)–(C). The general case (G3) was proved 2026-11-18.
- `limits-representables.md` §10.2: marking needs no closure condition.
- **G1** (re-check every [recall] claim): done 2026-11-18 by CONV. All ~35 claims in `internal-hom.md`, `pseudo-vs-gray.md`, `limits-representables.md`, `free-objects.md`, `invertibility-markings.md`, the conjecture files and the project-folder survey are now cited with their place, proved, or corrected. Corrections: Steiner's tensor statement is for *strongly* loop-free bases; Al-Agl–Steiner, not Crans, introduced the ω-Gray tensor, and no "pseudo version" was found; BMOSV's naturality is only for depth ≤ 1, so `internal-hom.md` §4.1's Γ ⊗ D¹ on composite terms depends on Conjecture N (C1); ω-sesquicategories are not Markakis's setting; the dimension-2 lax-limit theory is Cat-weighted, not Gray-weighted; no model structure on weak ω-categories exists to cite (now in E11).
- **G2** (one place for conventions): done 2026-11-18 by CONV, `conventions.md`. L's factor order (execution order, L = AM's ∞-Cat_lax), names checked against AM, JFS, Haugseng, Loubaton and Gepner–Heine (all agree with Bénabou's lax), Mikhail's transformations are Bénabou-oplax, the naming hazard between `internal-hom.md`'s lax hom (AM's Hom_oplax) and L's hom 𝒦(Θ, C) (AM's Hom_lax), and the duality table. Fixed one inconsistent factor order in `invertibility-markings.md` §4.2.
- **G3** (Steiner support lemma for arbitrary Steiner complexes): done 2026-11-18 by CONV. Problems 1–3 of `conjectures/steiner-support-determines-cells.md` hold for every Steiner complex. Problem 1 is Forest Lemma 3.4.5 / Campbell Prop. 2.20. Problem 2 follows from a lifting lemma (an element of the support of x lies in the top chain of some cell parallel to the relevant boundary of x) plus Forest Lemma 3.4.11 / Campbell Prop. 2.23. Problem 3 follows from Supp(x #_k y) = Supp(x) ∪ Supp(y). The convexity step (Conv) is no longer needed.
- C6 (weak model of ATTACH): answered in `free-objects-proofs.md` §4. Strict and (∞,ω) models justified; the algebraic-weak gap is part of E11.
- C4 (lifts of invertible cells are invertible): proved in `free-objects-proofs.md` §1 and in Agda (`src/CaTTExp/Free/Reflect.agda`, PR #28, reviewed).
- C9 (density): answered in `free-objects-proofs.md` §6. Disks are not dense, Θ is; the Θ-density rule is recorded under F5.
- B8 (oriented lax limits): answered by a researcher, verified 2026-11-18. Problems 1–2 yes; Problem 3 false as stated, true with coherent marking (full Hom_C(b,a)). The internal R11 re-derivation is done by the response's §1. Leaves B7.
- C1a (verify the response on N): done 2026-11-18. Ladder theorem verified; Lemma 2, §4 case 1, §8, orientation and path-object corrections applied. Leaves C1, C1b, D3a.
- F7 (ICaTT and Rice rules in full): done 2026-11-18, `icatt-and-rice-rules.md`.
- E6a (verify the response on directed interchange): done 2026-11-18. Raw-schema no-go verified; statement corrected; `pseudo-vs-gray.md` §2.5 and §7 updated. Leaves E6.
